\displaystyle N^{-1}\psi(x)1_{U_{1,k}}(x)\leq\Psi_{k}(x)\leq N\psi(x)1_{U_{3,k}}(x)

(3.10)

for almost every
x\in\Omega
, where
N=N(\mathrm{C}_{1}(\psi))
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\begin{split}|D^{\alpha}\Psi_{k}(x)|\,&\leq\|D^{\alpha}\zeta_{k}\|_{\infty}\int_{B(x,2^{k-4})}\psi 1_{U_{2,k}}\,\mathrm{d}y\\
&\leq 2^{-|\alpha|k}\big(\underset{B(x,\rho(x)/2)}{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}\psi\big)1_{U_{3,k}}(x)\\
&\leq N\rho(x)^{-|\alpha|}\psi(x)1_{U_{3,k}}(x)\end{split}

(3.11)

for almost every
x\in\Omega
, where
N=N(d,\alpha,\mathrm{C}_{1}(\psi))
(see (
3.8
) and Remark
3.4
).
Due to (
3.6
), (
3.10
), and (
3.11
),
\Psi:=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\Psi_{k}
belongs to
C^{\infty}(\Omega)
and

\displaystyle\Psi\simeq_{\mathrm{C}_{1}(\psi)}\psi\quad,\quad|D^{\alpha}\Psi|\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|D^{\alpha}\Psi_{k}|\lesssim_{N}\rho^{-|\alpha|}\psi

(3.12)

for almost every
x\in\Omega
, where
N=N(d,\alpha,\mathrm{C}_{1}(\psi))
.
By (
3.12
), the proof is completed.

(2) Let
x,y\in\Omega
satisfy
|x-y|<\rho(x)/2
.
For
r\in[0,1]
, put
x_{r}=(1-r)x+ry
, so that

x_{r}\in B\big(x,\rho(x)/2\big)\quad\text{and}\quad\rho(x_{r})\geq\rho(x)-|x-x_{r}|\geq|x-y|\,.

Then we have

\displaystyle\Psi(x_{r})\,

\displaystyle\leq\Psi(x_{0})+|x-y|\int_{0}^{r}\big|(\nabla\Psi)(x_{t})\big|\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\Psi(x_{0})+N_{0}|x-y|\int_{0}^{r}\rho(x_{t})^{-1}\Psi(x_{t})\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\Psi(x_{0})+N_{0}\int_{0}^{r}\Psi(x_{t})\,\mathrm{d}t\,,

where
N_{0}=N(d,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi))>0
.
By Grönwall’s inequality, we obtain

\Psi(y)=\Psi(x_{1})\leq e^{N_{0}}\Psi(x_{0})=e^{N_{0}}\Psi(x).

If
x,\,y\in\Omega
satisfy
|x-y|<\rho(x)/3
, then
|x-y|<\rho(x)/2
and
|x-y|<\rho(y)/2
.
Therefore we have

e^{-N_{0}}\Psi(y)\leq\Psi(x)\leq e^{N_{0}}\Psi(y)\,.

By Lemma
3.3
, the proof is completed.
∎

We end this subsection with the following remark, which describes the boundary behavior of regular Harnack functions on domains satisfying a certain geometric condition; this remark is used in Subsection
6.4

Remark 3.6
.

In
[
71
]
, the term ‘Harnack function’ is used not for the Harnack function defined in Definition
3.1
but for the continuous Harnack functions. It should be noted that regular Harnack functions are continuous Harnack functions.
If a domain is a John domain (which will be introduced later), then we obtain the upper and lower bounds of the boundary behavior of regular Harnack functions.

It follows from
[
71
, Corollary 3.4]
that for any domain
\Omega
, if
\Psi
is a regular Harnack function on
\Omega
, then

\displaystyle N_{0}^{-(k(x,x_{0})+1)}\leq\frac{\Psi(x)}{\Psi(x_{0})}\leq N_{0}^{k(x,x_{0})+1}\qquad\text{for all}\quad x_{0},\,x\in\Omega\,,

(3.13)

where
N_{0}\geq 1
is a constant depending only on
\mathrm{C}_{1}(\Psi)
, and
k(x,x_{0})\geq 0
is the quasihyperbolic distance between
x
and
x_{0}
(see
[
71
, paragraph 2.5]
for the definition). In addition, Gehring and Martio
[
21
, Theorem 3.11]
proved that if
\Omega
is a
John domain
, then for any
x_{0}\in\Omega
, there exists
N,\,A>0
depending only on
\Omega
and
x_{0}
such that

\displaystyle e^{k(x,x_{0})}\leq N\rho(x)^{-A}\qquad\text{whenever}\quad x\in\Omega\,.

(3.14)

Here,
\Omega
is called a John domain if the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

\Omega
is a connected and bounded open set.

(2)

There exist a point
x_{0}\in\Omega
and a constant
L_{0},\,\epsilon_{0}>0
such that for any
x\in\Omega
, there exists a rectifiable path
\gamma:\big[0,L\big]\rightarrow\Omega
parameterised by arclength such that
L\leq L_{0}
,
\gamma(0)=x
,
\gamma(L)=x_{0}
, and

d\big(\gamma(t),\partial\Omega\big)\geq\frac{\epsilon_{0}t}{L}\quad\text{for all}\,\,t\in[0,L]\,.

Due to (
3.13
) and (
3.14
), if
\Omega
is a John domain, then for any
x_{0}\in\Omega
, there exist constants
N,\,A>0
depending only on
\Omega
and
x_{0}
such that for any regular Harnack function
\Psi
on
\Omega
and
x\in\Omega
,

N^{-1}\rho(x)^{A}\leq\frac{\Psi(x)}{\Psi(x_{0})}\leq N\rho(x)^{-A}\,.

3.2.
Weighted Sobolev spaces and regular Harnack functions

In this subsection, we introduce the weighted Sobolev space
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
and generalize them through regular Harnack functions.

We first recall the definition of the Bessel potential space on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
For
p\in(1,\infty)
and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
,
H_{p}^{\gamma}=H_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
denotes the space of Bessel potential with the norm

\displaystyle\|f\|_{H_{p}^{\gamma}}:=\|(1-\Delta)^{\gamma/2}f\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}:=\big\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big[(1+|\xi|^{2})^{\gamma/2}\mathcal{F}(f)(\xi)\big]\big\|_{p}\,,

(3.15)

where
\mathcal{F}
is the Fourier transform and
\mathcal{F}^{-1}
is the inverse Fourier transform.
If
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then
H_{p}^{\gamma}
coincides with the Sobolev space

\displaystyle W_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):=\left\{f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\,:\,\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|D^{k}f|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x<\infty\right\}

(see,
e.g.
,
[
70
, Theorem 2.5.6]
).

We next introduce the weighted Sobolev spaces
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
and
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.
The space
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
was first introduced by Krylov
[
46
]
for
\Omega=\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
, and later generalized by Lototsky
[
57
]
for arbitrary domains
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
It is worth mentioning in advance that for
p\in(1,\infty)
,
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
and
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the space
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
coincides with the space

\displaystyle\left\{f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)\,:\,\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma}\int_{\Omega}|\rho^{k}D^{k}f|^{p}\rho^{\theta-d}\,\mathrm{d}x<\infty\right\}

(see
[
57
, Proposition 2.2.3]
or Lemma
3.8
of this paper).

In the remainder of this subsection, we assume that

\displaystyle p\in(1,\infty)\,,\,\,\,\gamma,\,\theta\in\mathbb{R}\,,\,\,\,\text{$\Psi$ is a regular Harnack function on $\Omega$}\,.

(3.16)

By
\widetilde{\rho}
we denote the regularization of
\rho=d(\,\cdot\,,\partial\Omega)
constructed in Lemma
3.5
.(1).
Recall that for each
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exists a constant
N_{k}=N(d,k)>0
such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rho}\simeq_{N_{0}}\rho\quad\text{and}\quad|D^{k}\widetilde{\rho}\,|\leq N_{k}\widetilde{\rho}^{\,1-k}\quad\text{on}\quad\Omega\,.

(3.17)

To define the weighted Sobolev spaces, fix a nonnegative function
\zeta_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that

\displaystyle\text{supp}(\zeta_{0})\subset[e^{-1},e]\,\,,\,\,\text{and}\quad\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\zeta_{0}(e^{n}t)=1\quad\text{for all}\,\,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\,.

For
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, put

\displaystyle\zeta_{0,(n)}(x)=\zeta_{0}\big(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(x)\big)1_{\Omega}(x)

(3.18)

so that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\zeta_{0,(n)}\equiv 1\quad\text{on}\,\,\Omega\,,\\
&\text{supp}(\zeta_{0,(n)})\subset\{x\in\Omega\,:\,e^{n-1}\leq\widetilde{\rho}(x)\leq e^{n+1}\}\,,\\
&\zeta_{0,(n)}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\quad\text{and}\quad|D^{\alpha}\zeta_{0,(n)}|\leq N(d,\alpha,\zeta)\,e^{-n|\alpha|}\,.\end{split}

(3.19)

Definition 3.7
.

(1)

By
H_{p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
we denote the class of all distributions
f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
such that

\displaystyle\|f\|^{p}_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}:=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}<\infty\,.

(2)

By
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
we denote the class of all distributions
f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
such that
f=\Psi g
for some
g\in H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
. The norm in
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
is defined by

\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}:=\|\Psi^{-1}f\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

We also denote

L_{p,\theta}(\Omega)=H_{p,\theta}^{0}(\Omega)\quad\text{and}\quad\Psi L_{p,\theta}(\Omega)=\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{0}(\Omega)\,.

The spaces
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
and
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
are independent of the choice of
\zeta_{0}
(see
[
57
, Proposition 2.2.4]
or Proposition
A.3
.(5) of this paper).
Therefore we ignore the dependence on
\zeta_{0}
.
Similar to
H_{p}^{\gamma}
and
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, for
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the space
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
has the following equivalent norm:

Lemma 3.8

(see Proposition
A.7
)
.

For any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|f\|^{p}_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{k}(\Omega)}\simeq_{N}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq k}\int_{\Omega}\big|\rho^{|\alpha|}D^{\alpha}f\big|^{p}\Psi^{-p}\rho^{\theta-d}\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

where
N=N(c,p,k,\theta,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi))
.

For the case
-\gamma\in\mathbb{N}
, an equivalent norm of
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
is introduced in Corollary
3.16
.

Remark 3.9
.

If
\Psi
is a regularization of a Harnack function
\psi
, then we have

\|f\|^{p}_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{k}(\Omega)}\simeq_{N}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq k}\int_{\Omega}\big|\rho^{|\alpha|}D^{\alpha}f\big|^{p}\psi^{-p}\rho^{\theta-d}\,\mathrm{d}x

where
N=N(d,p,k,\theta,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\mathrm{C}_{3}(\psi,\Psi))
.

The remainder of this subsection presents the properties of
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
that are used in Subsection
3.3
.
Specifically, we focus on the generalization from
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
to
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.
While the properties of
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
are provided in Appendix
A.1
, we list the properties of
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
in Lemma
3.10
, which are directly used in this subsection and Subsection
3.3
.

We denote

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}=\{d,\,p,\,\gamma,\,\theta\}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{I}^{\prime}=\{d,\,p,\,\gamma,\,\theta,\,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi)\}\,.

Lemma 3.10

(see Proposition
A.3
)
.

(1)

For any
s<\gamma
,

\|f\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{s}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I},s}\|f\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

(2)

For any
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\eta\big(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(e^{n}\cdot)\big)f(e^{n}\cdot)\|^{p}_{H^{\gamma}_{p}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I},\eta}\|f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}^{p}.

(3)

For any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\|\widetilde{\rho}^{\,s}f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\simeq_{\mathcal{I},s}\|f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+sp}(\Omega)}\,.

(4)

For any multi-index
\alpha
,

\|D^{\alpha}f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I},\alpha}\|f\|_{H^{\gamma+|\alpha|}_{p,\theta-|\alpha|p}(\Omega)}.

(5)

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
|\gamma|\leq k
. If
a\in C^{k}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
satisfies

\displaystyle|a|_{k}^{(0)}:=\sup_{\Omega}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq k}\rho^{|\alpha|}|D^{\alpha}a|<\infty\,\,,

then

\|af\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}}|a|_{k}^{(0)}\|f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}.

Remark 3.11
.

Lemma
3.10
also holds if
f
is replaced by
\Psi^{-1}f
.
Therefore Lemmas
3.10
.(1), (3), and (5) remain valid when
H_{\ast,\ast}^{\ast}(\Omega)
is replaced by
\Psi H_{\ast,\ast}^{\ast}(\Omega)
.

Lemma 3.12
.

(1)

C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
is dense in
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.

(2)

\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}
is a reflexive Banach space with the dual
\Psi^{-1}H_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)
, where

\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}=1\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\theta}{p}+\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{p^{\prime}}=d\,.

Moreover, for any
f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
, we have

N^{-1}\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\leq\sup_{g\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega),g\neq 0}\frac{\langle f,g\rangle}{\|g\|_{\Psi^{-1}H_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)}}\leq N\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}

where
N=N(\mathcal{I}^{\prime})
.

(3)

For any
k,\,l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\|\big(D^{k}\Psi\big)D^{l}f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\leq N\|\Psi f\|_{H^{\gamma+l}_{p,\theta-(k+l)p}(\Omega)}

where
N=N(\mathcal{I}^{\prime},l,k)>0
.

(4)

Let
\Phi
be a regular Harnack function on
\Omega
, and there exist a constant
N_{0}>0
such that

\Psi\leq N_{0}\Phi\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\Omega\,.

Then

\|\Psi f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\leq N\|\Phi f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}.

where
N=N(\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Phi),N_{0})>0
.

(5)

Let
p^{\prime}\in(1,\infty)
,
\gamma^{\prime},\,\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\Psi^{\prime}
be a regular Harnack function on
\Omega
,
if
f\in\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\cap\Psi^{\prime}H_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega)
, then there exists
\{f_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
such that

\|f-f_{n}\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}+\|f-f_{n}\|_{\Psi^{\prime}H_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as}\,\,\,n\rightarrow\infty\,.

Proof.

(1), (2) When
\Psi\equiv 1
, the results can be found in
[
57
]
(or see Proposition
A.2
of this paper).
Since the map
f\mapsto\Psi^{-1}f
is an isometric isomorphism from
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
to
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, there is nothing to prove.

(3) Since
\Psi
and
\widetilde{\rho}
are regular Harnack functions, we obtain that for any
k,\,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\Big|\frac{D^{k}\Psi}{\widetilde{\rho}^{\,-k}\Psi}\Big|^{(0)}_{m}\leq N(d,k,m,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi))\,.

By Lemma
3.10
.(5) and (3), we have

\displaystyle\|\big(D^{k}\Psi\big)f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime},k}\|\widetilde{\rho}^{\,-k}\Psi f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime},k}\|\Psi f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta-kp}(\Omega)}\,.

(3.20)

Therefore we only need to prove that for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|\Psi D^{l}f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime},l}\|\Psi f\|_{H^{\gamma+l}_{p,\theta-lp}(\Omega)}\,.

Recall that
\Psi^{-1}
is a regular Harnack function, and
\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi^{-1})
can be chosen to depend only on
\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi)
and
d
.
It follows from Leibniz’s rule, (
3.20
), and Lemma
3.10
.(4) and (1) that

\displaystyle\|\Psi D^{l}(\Psi^{-1}\Psi f)\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,l}

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{l}\|\Psi D^{l-n}(\Psi^{-1})\cdot D^{n}(\Psi f)\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{l}\|\Psi^{-1}\Psi D^{n}(\Psi f)\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta-(l-n)p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\|\Psi f\|_{H^{\gamma+l}_{p,\theta-lp}(\Omega)}\,.

(4) For any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

|\Psi\Phi^{-1}|^{(0)}_{k}\leq N(d,k,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Phi),N_{0})\,.

Therefore it follows from Lemma
3.10
.(5). that

\|\Psi f\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}=\|\Psi\Phi^{-1}(\Phi f)\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{N}\|\Phi f\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

(5) This follows from Lemma
A.6
.
∎

Remark 3.13
.

Lemmas
3.8
and
3.10
.(1) imply that
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
is continuously embedded in
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.
Due to Lemmas
3.12
.(1), (2), and that
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
is separable, we obtain that
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
is a separable and reflexive Banach space.

Remark 3.14
.

From Lemma
3.12
.(4), it follows thatfor regular Harnack functions
\Psi
and
\Phi
, if

N^{-1}\Phi\leq\Psi\leq N\Phi

for some constant
N>0
,
then
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
coincides with
\Phi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.
Therefore, applying Lemma
3.10
.(3), we see that if
\Psi
is a regularization of
\rho^{\sigma}
(
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
), then
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)=H_{p,\theta-\sigma p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.

Lemma 3.15
.

There exist linear maps

\Lambda_{0}\,:\,\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}\rightarrow\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)\quad\text{and}\quad\,\Lambda_{1},\,\ldots,\,\Lambda_{d}:\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}\rightarrow\Psi H_{p,\theta-p}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)

such that for any
f\in\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}f=\Lambda_{0}f+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}(\Lambda_{i}f)\quad\text{and}\\
\|\Lambda_{0}f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|\Lambda_{i}f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta-p}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.\end{gathered}

Proof.

It is provided in Lemma
A.5
that there exists linear maps

\widetilde{\Lambda}_{0},\,\ldots,\,\widetilde{\Lambda}_{d}\,:\,H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)

such that for any
g\in H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle g=\widetilde{\Lambda}_{0}g+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{i}g)\quad\text{and}\quad\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{0}g\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{i}g\|_{H_{p,\theta-p}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}}\|g\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

(3.21)

For
f\in\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}
(
\Leftrightarrow\Psi^{-1}f\in H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
), put

\displaystyle\Lambda_{0}f=\Psi\widetilde{\Lambda}_{0}(\Psi^{-1}f)-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\big(D_{i}\Psi\big)\times\widetilde{\Lambda}_{i}(\Psi^{-1}f)\,;

\displaystyle\Lambda_{i}f=\Psi\widetilde{\Lambda}_{i}(\Psi^{-1}f)\qquad\qquad\text{for}\,\,i=1,\,\ldots,\,d\,.

Then we have

\Big(\Lambda_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}\Lambda_{i}\Big)f=\Psi\Big(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{i}\Big)\big(\Psi^{-1}f\big)=f\,.

Moreover, Lemma
3.12
.(3) and (
3.21
) imply that

\displaystyle\|\Psi^{-1}\Lambda_{0}f\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|\Psi^{-1}\Lambda_{i}f\|_{H_{p,\theta-p}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{0}(\Psi^{-1}f)\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\Big(\|\Psi^{-1}D\Psi\cdot\widetilde{\Lambda}_{i}(\Psi^{-1}f)\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}+\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{i}(\Psi^{-1}f)\|_{H_{p,\theta-p}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}\,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{0}(\Psi^{-1}f)\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{i}(\Psi^{-1}f)\|_{H_{p,\theta-p}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}}\,

\displaystyle\|\Psi^{-1}f\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

Therefore the proof is completed.
∎

Corollary 3.16
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\simeq_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime},n}\inf\Big\{\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta-|\alpha|p}^{\gamma+n}(\Omega)}:\,f=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}D^{\alpha}f_{\alpha}\Big\}\,.

Proof.

Repeatedly applying Lemma
3.15
, we obtain linear maps

\Lambda_{n,\alpha}:\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\rightarrow\Psi H_{p,\theta-|\alpha|p}^{\gamma+n}(\Omega)\,,

indexed by multi-indices
\alpha
with
|\alpha|\leq n
, such that for any
f\in\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle f=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}D^{\alpha}\big(\Lambda_{n,\alpha}f\big)\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}\|\Lambda_{n,\alpha}f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta-|\alpha|p}^{\gamma+n}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime},n}\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

Therefore we obtain that for any
f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
,

\inf\Big\{\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta-|\alpha|p}^{\gamma+n}(\Omega)}:\,f=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}D^{\alpha}f_{\alpha}\Big\}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime},n}\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

For the inverse inequality, let
f=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}D^{\alpha}f_{\alpha}
where
f_{\alpha}\in H^{\gamma+n}_{p,\theta-|\alpha|p}(\Omega)
.
It follows from Lemma
3.12
.(2) and (3) that for any
g\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle|\langle f,g\rangle|\,

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}\big|\big\langle\Psi^{-1}f_{\alpha},\Psi D^{\alpha}g\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime},n}

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}\Big(\|\Psi^{-1}f_{\alpha}\|_{H_{\theta-|\alpha|p}^{\gamma+n}(\Omega)}\|\Psi D^{\alpha}g\|_{H_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}+|\alpha|p^{\prime}}^{-\gamma-n}(\Omega)}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime},n}\Big(

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}\|\Psi^{-1}f_{\alpha}\|_{H_{\theta-|\alpha|p}^{\gamma+n}(\Omega)}\Big)\|\Psi g\|_{H^{-\gamma}_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\,,

where
p^{\prime}=p/(p-1)
and
\theta^{\prime}/p^{\prime}=d-\theta/p
.
By taking the infimum over
\{f_{\alpha}\}_{|\alpha|\leq n}
and applying Lemma
3.12
.(2), we have

\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}^{\prime},n}\inf\Big\{\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{\Psi H^{\gamma+n}_{p,\theta-|\alpha|p}(\Omega)}:\,f=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}D^{\alpha}f_{\alpha}\Big\}\,.

Therefore the proof is completed.
∎

We end this subsection with Proposition
3.17
, which is a Sobolev-Hölder embedding theorem for the spaces
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.
This proposition is not used in Subsection
3.3
.
However, it provides Hölder estimates for solutions obtained in Theorem
3.18
.
For
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\alpha\in(0,1]
and
\delta\in\mathbb{R}
, we define the weighted Hölder norm

\displaystyle|f|^{(\delta)}_{k,\alpha}:=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\sup_{\Omega}\left|\rho^{\delta+i}D^{i}f\right|+\sup_{x,y\in\Omega}\frac{\left|\big(\widetilde{\rho}^{\delta+k+\alpha}D^{k}f\big)(x)-\big(\widetilde{\rho}^{\delta+k+\alpha}D^{k}f\big)(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\alpha}}\,.

Proposition 3.17
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\alpha\in(0,1]
.

(1)

For any
\delta\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\left|\Psi^{-1}f\right|^{(\delta)}_{k,\alpha}\simeq_{N}

\displaystyle\,\sum_{i=0}^{k}\sup_{x\in\Omega}\left|\Psi(x)^{-1}\rho(x)^{\delta+i}D^{i}f(x)\right|

\displaystyle+\sup_{x\in\Omega}\bigg(\Psi^{-1}(x)\rho^{\delta+k+\alpha}(x)\sup_{y:|y-x|<\frac{\rho(x)}{2}}\frac{\left|D^{k}f(x)-D^{k}f(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\alpha}}\bigg)\,,

where
N=N(d,k,\alpha,\delta,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi))
.

(2)

If
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
k+\alpha\leq\gamma-d/p
,
then for any
f\in\Psi H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\left|\Psi^{-1}f\right|^{(\theta/p)}_{k,\alpha}\leq N\|f\|_{\Psi H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\,,

where
N=N(\mathcal{I}^{\prime},k,\alpha)
.

Proof.

(1)
This result from the direct calculation and the definition of regular Harnack functions.
Therefore we leave the proof to the reader.

(2)
We only need prove for
\Psi\equiv 1
, and the result for this case is stated in
[
57
, Theorem 4.3]
.
We give a proof for the convenience of the reader.

For
f\in H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, the Sobolev embedding theorem implies

\displaystyle\left\|\big(f\zeta_{0,(n)}\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{C^{k,\alpha}}\leq N\left\|\big(f\zeta_{0,(n)}\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{H_{p}^{\gamma}}<\infty\,,

(3.22)

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,k,\delta)
.
Hence
f
belongs to
C^{k}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
.
For
x\in\Omega
, take
n_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
e^{n_{0}-1}\leq\rho(x)\leq e^{n_{0}}
.
If
|x-y|<\frac{\rho(x)}{2}
, then
e^{n_{0}-2}\leq\rho(y)\leq e^{n_{0}+2}
.
Take constants
A
and
B
depending only on
d
such that

\displaystyle A^{-1}\rho\leq\widetilde{\rho}\leq A\rho\,\,,\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{|n|\leq B}\zeta_{0}\big(e^{n}t\big)\equiv 1\quad\text{for all}\quad t\in[(Ae^{2})^{-1},Ae^{2}]\,.

Then we have

\sum_{|n-n_{0}|\leq B}\zeta_{0,(n)}\equiv 1\quad\text{on}\quad U_{n_{0}}:=\left\{y\,:\,e^{n_{0}-2}\leq\rho(y)\leq e^{n_{0}+2}\right\}.

Due to
B(x,\rho(x)/2)\subset U_{n_{0}}
and (
3.22
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left(\rho^{\theta/p+i}(x)\left|D^{i}f(x)\right|\right)+\rho^{\theta/p+k+\alpha}(x)\sup_{y:|y-x|<\frac{\rho(x)}{2}}\frac{\left|D^{k}f(x)-D^{k}f(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\,e^{n_{0}\theta/p}\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left|D^{i}\big(f(e^{n_{0}}\,\cdot\,)\big)(x)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sup_{y:e^{-{n_{0}}}y\in U_{n_{0}}}\frac{\left|D^{k}\big(f(e^{n_{0}}\,\cdot\,)\big)(x)-D^{k}\big(f(e^{n_{0}}\,\cdot\,)\big)(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\alpha}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{|n-n_{0}|\leq B}e^{n_{0}\theta/p}\left\|(f\zeta_{0,(n)})(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{C^{k,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\left\|(f\zeta_{0,(n)})(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{H_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}\right)^{1/p}\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\theta,k,\delta)
.
By (1) of this proposition, the proof is completed.
∎

3.3.
Solvability of the Poisson equation

Throughout this subsection, we assume (
3.16
).
The goal of this subsection is to prove the following theorem:

Theorem 3.18
.

Let

\Omega
admit the Hardy inequality (
1.3
);

\psi
be a superharmonic Harnack function on
\Omega
;

\mu\in(-1/p,1-1/p)
,

and suppose that
\Psi
is a regularization of
\psi
.
For any
\lambda\geq 0
and
f\in\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d+2p-2}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, the equation

\displaystyle\Delta u-\lambda u=f

(3.23)

has a unique solution
u
in
\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)}+\lambda\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d+2p-2}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\leq N\|f\|_{\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d+2p-2}^{\gamma}(\Omega)},

(3.24)

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\mu,\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega),\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\mathrm{C}_{3}(\psi,\Psi))
.

Recall that
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega)
is the constant in (
1.3
), and
\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi)
and
\mathrm{C}_{3}(\psi,\Psi)
are the constants in Definition
3.1
.

In Theorem
3.18
, one can take
\psi=\Psi=1_{\Omega}
.
Another example of
\psi
is introduced in Example
3.21
which is associated with the Green function, and valid for any domain admitting the Hardy inequality.

Remark 3.19
.

The spaces
\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)
and
\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d+2p-2}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
in Theorem
3.18
do not depend on the specific choice of
\Psi
among regularizations of
\psi
(see Remark
3.14
).
If we take
\Psi
as
\widetilde{\psi}
which is the regularization of
\psi
provided in Lemma
3.5
.(1),
then Theorem
3.18
can be reformulated without including
\Psi
, by taking
\Psi=\widetilde{\psi}
.
Indeed,
\mathrm{C}_{2}(\widetilde{\psi})
and
\mathrm{C}_{3}(\psi,\widetilde{\psi})
depend only on
d
and
\mathrm{C}_{1}(\psi)
, the constant
N
in (
3.24
) depends only on
d
,
p
,
\gamma
,
\mu
,
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega)
, and
\mathrm{C}_{1}(\psi)
.
Therefore,
Additionally, for the case
\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}
, equivalent norms of
\widetilde{\psi}^{\,\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)
and
\widetilde{\psi}^{\,\mu}H_{p,d+2p-2}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
are provided in Lemma
3.8
and Corollary
3.16
.

Remark 3.20
.

If
\mu\notin(-1/p,1-1/p)
, then Theorem
3.18
does not hold in general, as pointed out in
[
46
, Remark 4.3]
.
To observe this, consider
\Omega=(0,\pi)
,
\psi(x)=\Psi(x)=\sin x
, and
\gamma=0
, and refer Lemmas
3.8
and
3.22
.

For
\mu\geq 1-1/p
, we aim to prove the non-existence of solutions to the equation
\Delta u=f
in
\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)
, for any fixed
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
such that
f\leq 0
and
f\not\equiv 0
.
Assume that there exists
u_{1}\in\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)
such that
\Delta u_{1}=f
.
Since
\Omega=(0,\pi)
is bounded, we have

\displaystyle u_{1}\in\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)=H_{p,d-\mu p-2}^{2}(\Omega)\subset H_{p,d-p}^{2}(\Omega)=\Psi^{1-2/p}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)\,.

Let
u_{0}
be the classical solution of the equation

\Delta u=f\quad\text{on}\,\,\,(0,\pi)\quad;\quad u(0)=u(\pi)=0.

Then
u_{0}(x)\simeq\sin x
, which implies that

u_{0}\in L_{p,d-\widetilde{\mu}p-2}(\Omega)\,(=\Psi^{\widetilde{\mu}}L_{p,d-2}(\Omega))\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\widetilde{\mu}<1-1/p\,.

Due to Lemma
3.22
, we have

\displaystyle u_{0}\in\Psi^{\widetilde{\mu}}H^{2}_{p,d-2}(\Omega)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\widetilde{\mu}<1-1/p\,.

(3.25)

Since
u_{0},\,u_{1}\in\Psi^{1-2/p}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)
and
\Delta u_{1}=\Delta u_{0}=f
, by the uniqueness of solutions in
\Psi^{1-2/p}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)
(see Theorem
3.18
), we conclude that
u_{1}=u_{0}
, which implies
u_{1}\in\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)
.
However, this leads to a contradiction, since
\mu\geq 1-1/p
(see (
3.25
)).
Therefore, there are no solutions to the equation
\Delta u=f
in
\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)
.

If
\mu<-1/p
, then
1_{\Omega}
belong to
\Psi^{\mu}H^{2}_{p,d-2}(\Omega)
(see Lemma
3.8
).
Therefore the equation
\Delta u=0
has at least two solutions in
\Psi^{\mu}H^{2}_{p,d-2}(\Omega)
.

Consider the case
\mu=-1/p
.
For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, take
\zeta_{n}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
such that

1_{\left[\frac{2}{n},\pi-\frac{2}{n}\right]}\leq\zeta_{n}\leq 1_{\left[\frac{1}{n},\pi-\frac{1}{n}\right]}\quad\text{and}\quad\left|D^{k}\zeta_{n}\right|\leq N(k)n^{k}\,.

One can observe that

\log n\lesssim\left\|\zeta_{n}\right\|_{H_{p,d-1}^{2}(\Omega)}^{p}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\Delta\zeta_{n}\right\|_{L_{p,d+2p-1}(\Omega)}^{p}\lesssim 1

for all large enough
n
.
Therefore there is no constant
N
satisfying (
3.24
) for
\gamma=0
.

Example 3.21
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a domain admitting the Hardy inequality.
We claim that
\phi_{0}:=G_{\Omega}(x_{0},\,\cdot\,)\wedge 1
is a superharmonic Harnack function on
\Omega
, where
G_{\Omega}
is the Green function of the Poisson equation in
\Omega
(see Remark
2.13
for
G_{\Omega}
), and
x_{0}
is an arbitrary fixed point of
\Omega
. Note that

G_{\Omega}(x_{0},\,\cdot\,)
is a positive classical superharmonic function on
\Omega
;

G_{\Omega}(x_{0},\,\cdot\,)
is harmonic on
\Omega\setminus\{x_{0}\}
.

This implies that
\phi_{0}
is a classical superharmonic function on
\Omega
(see Proposition
5.5
.(1)).

For
x\in\Omega
, denote
B(x)=B\big(x,\rho(x)/8\big)
.
If
|x-x_{0}|>\rho(x)/4
, then
G_{\Omega}(x_{0},\,\cdot\,)
is harmonic on
B\big(x,\rho(x)/4\big)
.
By the Harnack inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{B(x)}\phi_{0}=\Big(\sup_{y\in B(x)}G_{\Omega}(x_{0},y)\Big)\wedge 1\lesssim_{d}\Big(\inf_{y\in B(x)}G_{\Omega}(x_{0},y)\Big)\wedge 1=\inf_{B(x)}\phi_{0}\,.

If
|x-x_{0}|\leq\rho(x)/4
, then
\rho(x)\leq 4\rho(x_{0})/3
, which implies
B(x)\subset B\big(x_{0},\rho(x_{0})/2\big)
.
By Proposition
2.4
.(3), there exists
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1]
such that
G(x_{0},\,\cdot\,)\geq\epsilon_{0}
on
B\big(x_{0},\rho(x_{0})/2\big)
.
Therefore we have

\displaystyle\sup_{B(x)}\phi_{0}

\displaystyle\leq 1\leq\epsilon_{0}^{-1}\inf_{B(x)}\phi_{0}\,.

Consequently,
\phi_{0}
is a superharmonic Harnack function on
\Omega
.

It is worth noting that
\phi_{0}
is the smallest positive classical superharmonic function, up to constant multiples. That is, if
\phi
is a positive classical superharmonic function on
\Omega
, then there exists
N_{0}=N(\phi,\Omega,x_{0})>0
such that
\phi_{0}\leq N_{0}\phi
on
\Omega
.
To prove this, we start by noting that
G_{\Omega}(x_{0},\cdot)
is continuous in
\partial B(x_{0},\rho(x_{0})/2)
and
\phi
has the minimum in
\partial B(x_{0},\rho(x_{0})/2)
. Indeed,
G_{\Omega}(x_{0},\cdot)
is harmonic and
\phi
is superharmonic in
\Omega\setminus\{x_{0}\}
.
Take
M\geq 1
such that

G_{\Omega}(x_{0},\cdot)\leq M\phi\quad\text{on}\quad\partial B(x_{0},\rho(x_{0})/2)\,.

Then we have

\phi_{0}\leq G_{\Omega}(x_{0},\cdot)\leq M\phi\quad\text{on}\quad\Omega\setminus B(x_{0},\rho(x_{0})/2)\,,

which follows from properties of
G_{\Omega}
(see
[
7
, Lemma 4.1.8]
).
In addition, we obtain that
\phi_{0}\leq 1\leq M_{1}\phi
on
B\big(x_{0},\rho(x_{0})/2\big)
, where
M_{1}^{-1}:=\min_{\overline{B}(x_{0},\rho(x_{0})/2)}\phi>0
.

To prove Theorem
3.18
, we need the following two lemmas; the proof of Theorem
3.18
is provided after the proof of Lemma
3.23
:

Lemma 3.22

(Higher order estimates)
.

Let
\lambda\geq 0
, and suppose that
u,\,f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
satisfy

\Delta u-\lambda u=f\,.

Then for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi H^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}+\lambda\|u\|_{\Psi H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}\leq N\left(\|u\|_{\Psi H^{s}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}+\|f\|_{\Psi H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}\right)\,,

(3.26)

where
N=N(d,p,\theta,\gamma,C_{2}(\Psi),s)
.

Proof.

We denote
\Phi=\Psi^{-1}
so that
\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Phi)
depends only on
d
and
\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi)
.

Step 1.
First, we consider the case
s\geq\gamma+1
.
We can certainly assume that

\|\Phi u\|_{H^{s}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}+\|\Phi f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}<\infty\,,

for if not, there is nothing to prove.
Since

\displaystyle\|\Phi u\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{d,p,s,\gamma}\|\Phi u\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{s}(\Omega)}

(see Lemma
3.10
.(1)), we only need to prove for
s=\gamma+1
.
Put

\displaystyle v_{n}(x)=\zeta_{0}\big(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(e^{n}x)\big)\Phi(e^{n}x)u(e^{n}x)\,.

(3.27)

Since

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\left\|v_{n}\right\|_{H_{p}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}=\|\Phi u\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\Omega)}^{p}<\infty\,,

we have
v_{n}\in H_{p}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Observe that

\displaystyle\Delta v_{n}-e^{2n}\lambda v_{n}=\widetilde{f}_{n}\,,

(3.28)

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{n}(x)=\,

\displaystyle e^{2n}\zeta_{0}\big(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(e^{n}x)\big)\big(\Phi f\big)(e^{n}x)

\displaystyle-e^{2n}\zeta_{0}\big(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(e^{n}x)\big)\big(\Phi\Delta u\big)(e^{n}x)+\Delta v_{n}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle e^{2n}\zeta_{0}\big(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(e^{n}x)\big)(\Phi f)(e^{n}x)

\displaystyle+2e^{n}\zeta_{0}^{\prime}\big(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(e^{n}x)\big)\big(\nabla\widetilde{\rho}\cdot\nabla(\Phi u)\big)(e^{n}x)

\displaystyle+2e^{2n}\zeta_{0}\big(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(e^{n}x)\big)\big(\nabla u\cdot\nabla\Phi\big)(e^{n}x)

\displaystyle+\zeta_{0}^{\prime\prime}\big(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(e^{n}x)\big)\big(|\nabla\widetilde{\rho}|^{2}\Phi u\big)(e^{n}x)

\displaystyle+e^{n}\zeta_{0}^{\prime}\big(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(e^{n}x)\big)\big((\Delta\widetilde{\rho})\Phi u\big)(e^{n}x)

\displaystyle+e^{2n}\zeta_{0}\big(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(e^{n}x)\big)\big((\Delta\Phi)u\big)(e^{n}x)\,.

Make use of Lemmas
3.10
.(1) - (3) and
3.12
.(3) to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\big\|\widetilde{f}_{n}\big\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}\\
\lesssim_{N}&\left\|\Phi f\right\|^{p}_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}+\left\|\widetilde{\rho}_{x}(\Phi u)_{x}\right\|^{p}_{H_{p,\theta+p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}+\left\|\Phi_{x}u_{x}\right\|^{p}_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\\
&+\left\|\widetilde{\rho}_{x}\widetilde{\rho}_{x}\Phi u\right\|^{p}_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}+\left\|\widetilde{\rho}_{xx}\Phi u\right\|^{p}_{H_{p,\theta+p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}+\left\|\Phi_{xx}u\right\|^{p}_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\\
\lesssim_{N}&\|\Phi f\|^{p}_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}+\|\Phi u\|^{p}_{H^{\gamma+1}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}<\infty\,,\end{split}

(3.29)

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\theta,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi))
.
Hence
\tilde{f}_{n}
belongs to
H^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Due to (
3.28
) and that
v_{n}\in H_{p}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\widetilde{f}_{n}\in H_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}V_{n}:=(1-\Delta)^{\gamma/2}v_{n}\in H_{p}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\,\,,\,\,\,\,F_{n}:=(1-\Delta)^{\gamma/2}\widetilde{f}_{n}\in L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\,;\\
\Delta V_{n}-(e^{2n}\lambda+1)V_{n}=F_{n}-V_{n}\,.\end{gathered}

It is implied by classical results for the Poisson equation in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
(see,
e.g.
,
[
49
, Theorem 4.3.8, Theorem 4.3.9]
)
that

\displaystyle\|v_{n}\|_{H_{p}^{\gamma+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+e^{2n}\lambda\|v_{n}\|_{H_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|V_{n}\right\|_{H_{p}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+e^{2n}\lambda\left\|V_{n}\right\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(3.30)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta V_{n}\right\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+(e^{2n}\lambda+1)\left\|V_{n}\right\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,p}

\displaystyle\|F_{n}-V_{n}\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}_{n}\|_{H_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|v_{n}\|_{H_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

Combine (
3.30
) and (
3.29
) to obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\Phi u\|_{H^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}^{p}+\lambda^{p}\|\Phi u\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}^{p}=\,\,\,\,&\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\Big(\|v_{n}\|_{H^{\gamma+2}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}+(e^{2n}\lambda)^{p}\|v_{n}\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}\Big)\\
\lesssim_{N}&\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\left(\|v_{n}\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}+\|\tilde{f}_{n}\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}\right)\\
\lesssim_{N}&\|\Phi u\|_{H^{\gamma+1}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}^{p}+\|\Phi f\|^{p}_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.\end{split}

Therefore the case
s=\gamma+1
is proved.
Consequently, (
3.26
) holds for all
s\geq\gamma+1
.

Step 2.
Recall that for the case
s\geq\gamma+1
, (
3.26
) is proved in Step 1.
For
s<\gamma+1
, take
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\gamma+1-k\leq s<\gamma+2-k\,,

and repeatedly apply (
3.26
) with
(\gamma,s)
replaced by
(\gamma,\gamma+1)
,
(\gamma-1,\gamma)
, …,
(\gamma-k,\gamma+1-k)
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\|\Phi u\|_{H^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}+\lambda\|\Phi u\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\|\Phi u\|_{H^{\gamma+1}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}+\|\Phi f\|_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\cdots

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\|\Phi u\|_{H^{\gamma-k+1}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}+\|\Phi f\|_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

Since
\|\Phi u\|_{H^{\gamma-k+1}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|\Phi u\|_{H^{s}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}
(see Lemma
3.10
.(1)), the proof is completed.
∎

Lemma 3.23
.

Let
\lambda\geq 0
, and suppose the following:

For any
f\in\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, in
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)
there exists a unique solution
u
of equation (
3.23
).
For this solution, we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)}+\lambda\|u\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\leq N_{\gamma}\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,,

(3.31)

where
N_{\gamma}
is a constant independent of
f
and
u
.

Then for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, the following holds:

For any
f\in\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{s}(\Omega)
, in
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{s+2}(\Omega)
there exists a unique solution
u
of equation (
3.23
).
For this solution, we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{s+2}(\Omega)}+\lambda\|u\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{s}(\Omega)}\leq N_{s}\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{s}(\Omega)}\,,

(3.32)

where
N_{s}
is a constant depends only on
d,\,p,\,\gamma,\,\theta,\,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\,N_{\gamma}
and
s
.

Proof.

To prove the uniqueness of solutions, let us assume that
\overline{u}\in\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{s+2}(\Omega)
satisfies
\Delta\overline{u}-\lambda\overline{u}=0
.
By Lemma
3.22
,
\overline{u}
belongs to
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)
.
Due to the assumption of this lemma, in
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)
, the zero distribution is the unique solution for the equation
\Delta u-\lambda u=0
.
Consequently,
\overline{u}
is also the zero distribution, and the uniqueness of solutions is proved.
Thus it remains to show the existence of solutions and estimate (
3.32
).

Step 1.
We first consider the case
s>\gamma
.
Let
f\in\Psi H^{s}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)
.
Due to
\Psi H^{s}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)\subset\Psi H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)
,
f
belongs to
\Psi H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)
, and hence there exists a solution
u\in\Psi H^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)
of equation (
3.23
).
It follows from Lemma
3.22
, (
3.31
), and Lemma
3.10
.(1) that

\displaystyle\left\|u\right\|_{\Psi H^{s+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}+\lambda\left\|u\right\|_{\Psi H^{s}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\left\|u\right\|_{\Psi H^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}+\left\|f\right\|_{\Psi H^{s}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N_{\gamma}\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}+\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{s}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle(N_{\gamma}+1)\left\|f\right\|_{\Psi H^{s}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\theta,\gamma,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),s)
.
Therefore
u
belongs to
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{s+2}(\Omega)
, and the proof is completed.

Step 2.
Consider the case
s<\gamma
.
Since the case
s\geq\gamma
is proved in Step 1, by mathematical induction, it is sufficient to show that if this lemma holds for
s=s_{0}+1
, then this also holds for
s=s_{0}
.

Let us assume that this lemma holds for
s=s_{0}+1
.
For
f\in\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{s_{0}}(\Omega)
, by Lemma
3.15
, there exists

f^{0}\in\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{s_{0}+1}(\Omega)\quad\text{and}\quad f^{1},\,\ldots,\,f^{d}\in\Psi H_{p,\theta+p}^{s_{0}+1}(\Omega)

such that
f=f^{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}f^{i}
and

\displaystyle\left\|f^{0}\right\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{s_{0}+1}(\Omega)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\|\widetilde{\rho}^{\,-1}f^{i}\right\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{s_{0}+1}(\Omega)}\leq N\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{s_{0}}(\Omega)}\,,

(3.33)

where
N=N(d,p,\theta,s_{0},\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi))
.
Due to the assumption that this lemma holds for
s=s_{0}+1
, there exist
v^{0},\,\cdots,\,v^{d}\in\Psi H_{p,d-2}^{s_{0}+3}(\Omega)
such that

\displaystyle\Delta v^{0}-\lambda v^{0}=f^{0}\quad\text{and}\quad\Delta v^{i}-\lambda v^{i}=\widetilde{\rho}^{\,-1}f^{i}\quad\text{for }i=1,\,\ldots,\,d\,,

and

\displaystyle\left\|v^{0}\right\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+3}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}+\lambda\left\|v^{0}\right\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+1}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\left\|v^{i}\right\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+3}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}+\lambda\left\|v^{i}\right\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+1}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N_{s_{0}+1}\bigg(\left\|f^{0}\right\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+1}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\|\widetilde{\rho}^{\,-1}f^{i}\right\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+1}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}\bigg)

(3.34)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\,

\displaystyle N_{s_{0}+1}\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{s_{0}}(\Omega)}\,,

where the last inequality follows from (
3.33
).
Put
v=v^{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}\big(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i}\big)
, and observe that

\Delta v-\lambda v=f+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}\big(\Delta(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i})-\widetilde{\rho}\Delta v^{i}\big)\,.

By Lemmas
3.10
and
3.12
.(3), we have

\displaystyle\left\|D_{i}\left(\Delta(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i})-\widetilde{\rho}\Delta v^{i}\right)\right\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+1}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\,

\displaystyle\|\Delta(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i})-\widetilde{\rho}\Delta v^{i}\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+2}_{p,\theta+p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{d}\left(\|D_{kk}\widetilde{\rho}\cdot v^{i}\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+2}_{p,\theta+p}(\Omega)}+\|D_{k}\widetilde{\rho}\cdot D_{k}v^{i}\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+2}_{p,\theta+p}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\,

\displaystyle\|v^{i}\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+3}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}<\infty\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\theta,s_{0},\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi))
.
Due to the assumption that this lemma holds for
s=s_{0}+1
, there exists
w\in\Psi H^{s_{0}+3}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)
such that

\Delta w-\lambda w=\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}\big(\Delta(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i})-\widetilde{\rho}\Delta v^{i}\big)\quad(=\Delta v-\lambda v-f),

and

\displaystyle\|w\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+3}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}+\lambda\|w\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+1}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}

(3.35)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N_{s_{0}+1}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\|D_{i}\left(\Delta(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i})-\widetilde{\rho}\Delta v^{i}\right)\right\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+1}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\,

\displaystyle N_{s_{0}+1}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|v^{i}\|_{\Psi H^{s_{0}+3}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\,.

Put

u=v-w=v^{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}(\widetilde{\rho}v^{i})-w\,.

Then
u
satisfies
\Delta u-\lambda u=f
.
Moreover, by (
3.3
) and (
3.35
), we obtain (
3.32
) for
s=s_{0}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.18
.

By Lemma
3.23
, we only need to prove for
\gamma=0
.

A priori
estimates.
Assume that
u\in\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)
and
\Delta u-\lambda u\in\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega)
.
By Lemma
3.22
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)}+\lambda\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega)}\\
\lesssim_{N}&\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d-2}(\Omega)}+\|\Delta u-\lambda u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega)}<\infty\,,\end{split}

(3.36)

where
N=N(d,p,\mu,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi))
.
Due to (
3.36
) and Lemma
3.12
.(5), whether
\lambda=0
or
\lambda>0
, there exists
u_{n}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
such that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\|u-u_{n}\|_{\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)}+\lambda\|u-u_{n}\|_{\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega)}\Big)=0\,.

This implies

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\big\|\big(\Delta-\lambda\big)(u-u_{n})\big\|_{\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega)}=0\,.

Since
\Psi
is a regularization of the superharmonic Harnack function
\psi
, Theorem
2.11
and Lemma
3.8
imply

\displaystyle\|u_{n}\|_{\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d-2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\simeq_{N}\int_{\Omega}|u_{n}|^{p}\psi^{-\mu p}\rho^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}x

(3.37)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\int_{\Omega}|\Delta u_{n}-\lambda u_{n}|^{p}\psi^{-\mu p}\rho^{2p-2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\simeq_{N}\|\Delta u_{n}-\lambda u_{n}\|_{\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega)}\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\mu,\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega),\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),\mathrm{C}_{3}(\psi,\Psi))
.
By letting
n\rightarrow\infty
, we obtain (
3.37
) for
u
instead of
u_{n}
.
By combining this with (
3.36
), we obtain the
a priori
estimates,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\,&\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)}+\lambda\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega)}\\
\lesssim_{N}\,&\|u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d-2}(\Omega)}+\|\Delta u-\lambda u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega)}\\
\lesssim_{N}\,&\|\Delta u-\lambda u\|_{\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega)}\,.\end{split}

(3.38)

Note that (
3.38
) also implies the uniqueness of solutions.

Existence of solutions.

Since
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
is dense in
\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega)
, for
f\in\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega)
there exists
f_{n}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
such that
f_{n}\rightarrow f
in
\Psi^{\mu}L_{d,d+2p-2}(\Omega)
.
Lemmas
2.12
and
3.8
yield that for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
u_{n}\in\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)
such that

\Delta u_{n}-\lambda u_{n}=f_{n}\,.

Due to Lemma
3.22
,
u_{n}\in\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)
.
Since
f_{n}\rightarrow f
in
\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}(\Omega)
, it follows from (
3.38
) that

\|u_{n}-u_{m}\|_{\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq N\|f_{n}-f_{m}\|_{\Psi^{\mu}L_{p,d+2p-2}}\rightarrow 0

as
n,\,m\rightarrow\infty
.
Therefore there exists
u\in\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)
such that
u_{n}
converges to
u
in
\Psi^{\mu}H_{p,d-2}^{2}(\Omega)
.
Since
u_{n}
and
f_{n}
converge to
u
and
f
in the sense of distribution, respectively (see Lemma
3.12
.(2)),
u
is a solution of equation (
3.23
).
∎

We end this subsection with a global uniqueness of solutions.

Theorem 3.24

(Global uniqueness)
.

Suppose that (
1.3
) holds for
\Omega
, and for each
i=1,\,2
,
\Psi_{i}
is a regularlization of a superharmonic Harnack function,
p_{i}\in(1,\infty)
,
\gamma_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\mu_{i}\in(-1/p_{i},1-1/p_{i})
.
For
f\in\bigcap_{i=1,2}\Psi_{i}^{\mu_{i}}H_{p_{i},d+2p_{i}-2}^{\gamma_{i}}(\Omega)
and
i=1,\,2
, let
u^{(i)}\in\Psi_{i}^{\mu_{i}}H_{p_{i},d-2}^{\gamma_{i}+2}(\Omega)
be solutions of the equation

\displaystyle\Delta u-\lambda u=f\,.

Then
u^{(1)}=u^{(2)}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.12
.(5), there exist
\{f_{n}\}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
such that

f_{n}\rightarrow f\quad\text{in}\quad\bigcap_{i=1,2}\Psi_{i}^{\mu_{i}}H_{p_{i},d+2p_{i}-2}^{\gamma_{i}}(\Omega)\,.

By Lemmas
2.12
and
3.8
, for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
u_{n}\in\bigcap\limits_{i=1,2}\Psi_{i}^{\mu_{i}}L_{p_{i},d-2}(\Omega)
such that

\Delta u_{n}-\lambda u_{n}=f_{n}\,.

Lemma
3.22
yields that
u_{n}\in\bigcap\limits_{i=1,2}\Psi_{i}^{\mu_{i}}H_{p_{i},d-2}^{\gamma_{i}+2}(\Omega)
.
Since

(\Delta-\lambda)\big(u_{n}-u^{(1)}\big)=(\Delta-\lambda)\big(u_{n}-u^{(2)}\big)=f_{n}-f\,,

Theorem
3.18
implies that

u_{n}\rightarrow u^{(1)}\,\,\,\,\text{in}\,\,\,\,\Psi_{1}^{c_{1}}H_{p_{1},d-2}^{\gamma_{1}+2}(\Omega)\,\,,\,\,\text{and}\quad u_{n}\rightarrow u^{(2)}\,\,\,\,\text{in}\,\,\,\,\Psi_{2}^{c_{2}}H_{p_{2},d-2}^{\gamma_{2}+2}(\Omega)\,.

Consequently, by Lemma
3.12
.(2),

\langle u^{(1)},g\rangle=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\langle u_{n},g\rangle=\langle u^{(2)},g\rangle

for all
g\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
.
∎

4.
Parabolic equations

For
0<\nu_{1}\leq\nu_{2}<\infty
and
T\in(0,\infty]
, we denote

•

\mathrm{M}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
: the set of all
d\times d
real-valued symmetric matrices
(\alpha^{ij})_{d\times d}
satisfying

\nu_{1}|\xi|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\alpha^{ij}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\leq\nu_{2}|\xi|^{2}\qquad\forall\,\,\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d};

•

\mathcal{M}_{T}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
: the set of all
\mathcal{L}:=\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}a^{ij}(\cdot)D_{ij}
, where
\{a^{ij}(\cdot)\}_{i,j=1,....,d}
is a family of time measurable function on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that
\big(a^{ij}(t)\big)_{d\times d}\in\mathrm{M}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
for all
t\in(0,T]
.

Throughout this section we assume that
\Omega
be a domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle T\in(0,\infty]\,\,,\,\,\,\,0<\nu_{1}\leq\nu_{2}<\infty\,\,,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\,\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{M}_{T}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})\,,

(4.1)

and use the convention that
(0,T]=(0,\infty)
and
[0,T]=[0,\infty)
if
T=\infty
.
We deal with the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u=\mathcal{L}u+f:=\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}a^{ij}(t)D_{ij}u+f\,\,,\quad t\in(0,T]\quad;\quad u(0,\cdot)=u_{0}\,,

(4.2)

repeating the arguments in Sections
2
and
3
.

4.1.
Key estimates for parabolic equations

Unlike
\Delta
, the operator
\mathcal{L}
in (
4.2
) consists of variable coefficients.
Hence Lemma
2.8
.(2) is not applied directly.
For this reason we introduce the following definition:

Definition 4.1
.

Let
\phi
be a positive superharmonic function on
\Omega
.
For
\delta\in(0,1]
and
p\in(1,\infty)
, by
I(\phi,p,\delta)
we denote the set of all constants
\mu\in(-\frac{1}{p},1-\frac{1}{p})
satisfying the following: there exists a constant
\mathrm{C}_{4}>0
such that the inequality

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\cap\{u\neq 0\}}|u|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\mathrm{C}_{4}\int_{\Omega}\big(-\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\alpha^{ij}D_{ij}u\big)\cdot u|u|^{p-2}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.3)

holds for all
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
and
(\alpha^{ij})_{d\times d}\in\mathrm{M}(\delta,1)
.

We employ the set
I(\phi,p,\delta)
to state the main theorems in this section, specifically Theorems
4.3
and
4.15
.
According to Lemma
2.8
, when
\delta=1
,
I(\phi,p,1)
coincides with
(-1/p,1-1/p)
.
Notably, even for
\delta\in(0,1)
and without additional assumptions on
\Omega
,
\phi
, and
p
, the following proposition guarantees the existence of a non-empty interval contained in
I(\phi,\delta,p)
.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
\phi
be a positive superharmonic function on
\Omega
,
p\in(1,\infty)
, and
\delta\in(0,1]
.

(1)

If

\displaystyle\mu\in\Big(-\frac{(p-1)/p}{p(\delta^{-1/2}+1)/2-1},\frac{(p-1)/p}{p(\delta^{-1/2}-1)/2+1}\Big)\,,

(4.4)

then
\mu\in I(\phi,p,\delta)
, and the constant
\mathrm{C}_{4}
in (
4.3
) can be chosen to depend only on
\delta,p
and
\mu
.
In particular,
I(\phi,p,\delta)=(-1+1/p,1/p)
.

(2)

Suppose that for any
(\alpha^{ij})_{d\times d}\in\mathrm{M}(\delta,1)
,
\sum\limits_{i,j=1}^{d}\alpha^{ij}D_{ij}\phi\leq 0
in the sense of distribution.
then

I(\phi,\delta,p)=\big(-1/p,1-1/p\big)\,.

Moreover for any
\mu\in\big(-1/p,1-1/p\big)
, the constant
\mathrm{C}_{4}
in (
4.3
) can be chosen to depend only on
d,\delta,p
and
\mu
.

Proposition
4.2
.(2) is used for results on convex domains and domains satisfying the totally vanishing exterior Reifenberg condition; see Subsections
6.2
and
6.3
, respectively.

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

(1) Let
\mu
satisfy (
4.4
). By the same argument as in the beginning of the proof of Lemma
2.8
.(2) and (3), it sufficies to prove (
4.3
) only for
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
and a positive smooth superharmonic function
\phi
on a neighborhood of
\text{supp}(u)
.

Put
c=-\mu p\in(-p+1,1)
and
v=u\phi^{c/(2p-2)}
.
Due to Lemmas
2.9
,
2.8
.(1), and that
(\alpha^{ij})\in\mathrm{M}(\delta,1)
, we have

\displaystyle-\sum_{i,j}\int_{\Omega}\alpha^{ij}u_{ij}|u|^{p-2}u\phi^{c}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle(p-1)\sum_{i,j}\int_{\Omega}\alpha^{ij}u_{i}u_{j}|u|^{p-2}\phi^{c}dx+c\sum_{i,j}\int_{\Omega}\alpha^{ij}|u|^{p-2}uu_{i}\phi_{j}\phi^{c-1}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle(p-1)\int_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i,j}\alpha^{ij}v_{i}v_{j}\Big)|v|^{p-2}\phi^{c^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{c^{2}}{4(p-1)}\int_{\Omega}|v|^{p}\Big(\sum_{i,j}\alpha^{ij}\phi_{i}\phi_{j}\Big)\phi^{c^{\prime}-2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle(p-1)\delta\int_{\Omega}|\nabla v|^{2}|v|^{p-2}\phi^{c^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{c^{2}}{4(p-1)}\int_{\Omega}|v|^{p}|\nabla\phi|^{2}\phi^{c^{\prime}-2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle\kappa^{\prime}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla v|^{2}|v|^{p-2}\phi^{c^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

where
c^{\prime}:=\frac{(p-2)c}{2p-2}\in(-p+1,1)
and

\kappa^{\prime}=\delta(p-1)-\frac{1}{4(p-1)}\Big(\frac{pc}{1-c^{\prime}}\Big)^{2}\,.

One can observe that
\kappa^{\prime}>0
if and only if
\mu
satisfies (
4.4
).
Therefore we only need to show that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|u|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}\phi^{c}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq N(p,\mu)\int_{\Omega}|v|^{p-2}|\nabla v|^{2}\phi^{c^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

(4.5)

Note that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\cap\{u\neq 0\}}|v|^{p-2}|\nabla v|^{2}\phi^{c^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\cap\{u\neq 0\}}|u|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}\phi^{c}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{c}{p-1}\int_{\Omega\cap\{u\neq 0\}}|u|^{p-2}u(\nabla u\cdot\nabla\phi)\phi^{c-1}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

If
c\in[0,1)
, then
\Delta(\phi^{c})\leq 0
on
\text{supp}(u)
(see (
2.8
)), which implies

\frac{c}{p-1}\int_{\Omega\cap\{u\neq 0\}}|u|^{p-2}u(\nabla u\cdot\nabla\phi)\phi^{c-1}\,\mathrm{d}x=-\frac{1}{p(p-1)}\int_{\Omega}|u|^{p}\Delta(\phi^{c})\,\mathrm{d}x\geq 0\,.

Therefore (
4.5
) holds.

If
c\in(-p+1,0)
, then Lemma
2.8
.(1) implies

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\cap\{u\neq 0\}}|u|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}\phi^{c}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{c}{p-1}\int_{\Omega\cap\{u\neq 0\}}|u|^{p-2}u(\nabla u\cdot\nabla\phi)\phi^{c-1}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\cap\{u\neq 0\}}|u|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}\phi^{c}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\frac{c}{p-1}\Big(\int_{\Omega\cap\{u\neq 0\}}|u|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}\phi^{c}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\int_{\Omega}|u|^{p}\phi^{c-2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle\frac{p-1+c}{(p-1)(1-c)}\int_{\Omega\cap\{u\neq 0\}}|u|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}\phi^{c}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Since
p-1+c>0
, the proof is completed.

(2) For a fixed
\mathrm{A}=(\alpha^{ij})_{d\times d}\in\mathrm{M}(\delta,1)
, take
\mathrm{B}\in\mathrm{M}(\sqrt{\delta},1)
such that
\mathrm{B}^{2}=\mathrm{A}
.
We denote
u_{\mathrm{B}}(y)=u(\mathrm{B}y)
and
\phi_{\mathrm{B}}(y)=\phi(\mathrm{B}y)
.
Since

\Delta\phi_{\mathrm{B}}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\alpha^{ij}\big(D_{ij}\phi\big)(\mathrm{B}\,\cdot\,)\leq 0

on
\mathrm{B}^{-1}\Omega:=\{\mathrm{B}^{-1}x\,:\,x\in\Omega\}
(in the sense of distribution),
Lemma
2.8
.(2) implies that for any
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\cap\{u\neq 0\}}|u|^{p-2}|\nabla u|^{2}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.6)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta^{-1}\int_{\mathrm{B}^{-1}\Omega\cap\{u_{\mathrm{B}}\neq 0\}}|u_{\mathrm{B}}|^{p-2}|\nabla u_{\mathrm{B}}|^{2}\phi_{\mathrm{B}}^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p,\mu,\delta}\,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathrm{B}^{-1}\Omega}(-\Delta u_{\mathrm{B}})\cdot u_{\mathrm{B}}|u_{\mathrm{B}}|^{p-2}\phi_{\mathrm{B}}^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\det(\mathrm{B}^{-1})\int_{\Omega}\big(-\alpha^{ij}D_{ij}u\big)\cdot u|u|^{p-2}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Since
\det(\mathrm{B}^{-1})=\big(\det(\mathrm{A})\big)^{-1/2}\in[1,\delta^{-d/2}]
, it follows that the last term in (
4.6
) is positive, and thus the proof is completed.
∎

Theorem
4.3
and Lemma
4.4
are counterparts of Theorem
2.11
and Lemma
2.12
, respectively.

Theorem 4.3
.

Suppose that

\displaystyle\text{$\Omega$ admits the Hardy inequality \eqref{hardy}}\,;

\displaystyle\text{$\phi$ is a positive superharmonic function on $\Omega$}\,;

\displaystyle\text{$p\in(1,\infty)$, and $\mu\in I(\phi,p,\nu_{1}/\nu_{2})$}\,.

Then for any
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,T]\times\Omega\big)
and
f:=\partial_{t}u-\mathcal{L}u
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\Omega}|u(t,\,\cdot)|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|u|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\rho^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\\
\leq\,&N\left(\int_{\Omega}|u(0,\cdot)|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|f|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\rho^{2p-2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\right)\,,\end{split}

(4.7)

where
N=N(p,\mu,\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega),\mathrm{C}_{4})
.

Proof.

For a fixed
t_{0}\in(0,T]
and
\epsilon>0
, integrate

p\,\big(\partial_{t}u\big)u|u|^{p-2}\,\phi^{-\mu p}-p\,\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}a^{ij}(t)D_{ij}u\cdot u|u|^{p-2}\phi^{-\mu p}=pf\cdot u|u|^{p-2}\phi^{-\mu p}

over
(0,t_{0}]\times\Omega
, and apply Young’s inequality, to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|u(t_{0},\,\cdot)|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+p\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\int_{\Omega}\Big(-\sum_{i,j}a^{ij}(t)D_{ij}u\Big)u|u|^{p-2}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

(4.8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|u(t_{0},\,\cdot)|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{-p+1}\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\int_{\Omega}|f|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\rho^{2p-2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+(p-1)\epsilon\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\int_{\Omega}|u|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\rho^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\,,

for any
\epsilon>0
.
Due to Lemma
2.8
.(3) and that
\mu\in I(\phi,p,\nu_{1}/\nu_{2})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\int_{\Omega}|u|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\rho^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p,\mu,\mathrm{C}_{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{2}|u|^{p-2}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

(4.9)

\displaystyle\leq_{\nu_{2},\mathrm{C}_{4}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\int_{\Omega}\Big(-\sum_{i,j}a^{ij}(t)D_{ij}u\Big)u|u|^{p-2}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\,.

By combining (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), taking the supremum over
t_{0}\in(0,T]
, and choosing a small enough
\epsilon>0
, we obtain (
4.7
); note that since
\phi^{-\mu p}
is locally integrable (see Proposition
2.4
), the first term in (
4.9
) is finite.
∎

Recall that
\langle F,\zeta\rangle
is the result of application of
F\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
to
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
.

Lemma 4.4

(Existence of a weak solution)
.

Let
\Omega
admit the Hardy inequality (
1.3
). For any
u_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
and
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,T]\times\Omega\big)
, there exists a measurable function
u:[0,T]\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfying the following:

(1)

u(t,\,\cdot\,)\in L_{1,\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
for each
t\in[0,T]
, and
u\in L_{1,\mathrm{loc}}([0,T]\times\Omega)
.

(2)

For any
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
and
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\big\langle u(t,\cdot),\zeta\big\rangle=\langle u_{0},\zeta\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\big\langle\Delta u(s,\cdot)+f(s,\cdot),\zeta\big\rangle\,\,\mathrm{d}s\,.

(4.10)

(3)

For any
p\in(1,\infty)
,
\mu\in(-1/p,1-1/p)
and positive superharmic function
\phi
on
\Omega
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\Omega}|u(t,\,\cdot\,)|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|u|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\rho^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\\
&\leq N\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|f|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\rho^{2p-2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{\Omega}|u_{0}|^{p}\phi^{-\mu p}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)\end{split}

(4.11)

where
N=N(p,c,\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega))
.

Proof.

We repeat the argument of Lemma
2.12
.
Take a sequence of infinitely smooth bounded domains
\{\Omega_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\text{supp}(u_{0})\subset\Omega_{1}\,\,,\quad\text{supp}(f)\subset[0,T]\times\Omega_{1}\,\,,\quad\overline{\Omega_{n}}\subset\Omega_{n+1}\,\,,\quad\bigcup_{n}\Omega_{n}=\Omega\,.

For
h\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega_{1})
and
H\in C_{c}^{\infty}([0,T]\times\Omega_{1})
, by
R_{n}(h,H)
we denote the classical solution
U\in C^{\infty}\big([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega_{n}}\big)
of the equation

\partial_{t}U=\Delta U+H1_{\Omega_{1}}\quad\text{on}\,\,(0,T]\times\Omega_{n}\quad;\quad U|_{[0,T]\times\partial\Omega_{n}}\equiv 0\quad\text{and}\quad U(0,\cdot\,)=h1_{\Omega_{1}}\,.

We first claim that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\Omega}|R_{n}(h,H)(t,\,\cdot\,)1_{\Omega_{n}}|^{p}\phi^{c}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|R_{n}(h,H)1_{\Omega_{n}}|^{p}\rho^{-2}\phi^{c}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\leq N(p,c,\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega))\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|H|^{p}\phi^{c}\rho^{2p-2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{\Omega}|h|^{p}\phi^{c}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)\,,\end{split}

(4.12)

for all
p\in(1,\infty)
,
c\in(-p+1,1)
and positive superharmonic functions
\phi
.
Note that
\overline{\Omega_{n}}
is a compact subset of
\Omega
, and for each
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle R_{n}(h,H)(t,\cdot\,)\in C^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega_{n}})\,\,,\,\,R_{n}(h,H)(t,\cdot\,)|_{\partial\Omega_{n}}\equiv 0\,,

which implies that
R_{n}(h,H)(t,\,\cdot\,)1_{\Omega_{n}}
satisfies condition (
2.3
).
If
T<\infty
, then we can repeat the proof of Theorem
4.3
for
R_{n}(h,H)1_{\Omega_{n}}
in place of
u
, using Lemma
2.8
.
This gives us (
4.12
).
For the case
T=\infty
, we first obtain (
4.12
) for
K\in(0,\infty)
instead of
T
.
Then, by letting
K\rightarrow\infty
, we obtain (
4.12
) even for the case
T=\infty
.

Take
U_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
and
F\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,T]\times\Omega\big)
such that
|u_{0}|\leq U_{0}
and
|f|\leq F
(recall that
[0,T]:=[0,\infty)
when
T=\infty
), and put

u_{0}^{1}=\frac{U_{0}+u_{0}}{2}\,\,,\quad u_{0}^{2}=\frac{U_{0}-u_{0}}{2}\,\,,\quad f^{1}=\frac{F+f}{2}\,\,,\quad f^{2}=\frac{F-f}{2}\,.

so that these functions are nonnegative,
u_{0}=u_{0}^{1}-u_{0}^{2}
, and
f=f^{1}-f^{2}
.

For
v_{n}:=R_{n}(u_{0}^{1},f^{1})1_{\Omega_{n}}
, the maximum principle implies that

0\leq v_{n}\leq v_{n+1}\quad\text{on}\quad[0,T]\times\Omega\,.

We denote the pointwise limit of
v_{n}
by
v
.
Apply the monotone convergence theorem to (
4.12
) with
(h,H,\phi,p,c)=(u_{0}^{1},f^{1},1_{\Omega},2,0)
to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\Omega}|v(t,\,\cdot\,)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|v|^{2}\rho^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|f^{1}|^{2}\rho^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{\Omega}|u_{0}^{1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

This implies that
v(t,\cdot\,)\in L_{1,\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
for each
t\in(0,T]
, and
v\in L_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\big([0,T]\times\Omega\big)
.

We next claim that for any
t\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle\big\langle v(t,\cdot),\zeta\big\rangle=\langle u_{0}^{1},\zeta\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\big\langle\Delta v(s,\cdot)+f^{1}(s,\cdot),\zeta\big\rangle\,\mathrm{d}s\,.

(4.13)

For a fixed
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
, take
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)\subset\Omega_{N}
.
Since
v_{n}:=R_{n}(u_{0}^{1},f^{1})1_{\Omega_{n}}
, we obtain that for any
n\geq N
,

\int_{\Omega}v_{n}(t,\cdot)\zeta\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}u_{0}^{1}\,\zeta\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Big(v_{n}(s,\cdot)\Delta\zeta+f^{1}(s,\cdot)\zeta\Big)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}s\,.

Since
0\leq v_{n}\leq v
,
v(t,\cdot)\in L_{1,\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
for each
t\in[0,T]
, and
v\in L_{1,\mathrm{loc}}([0,T]\times\Omega)
, the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem implies (
4.13
).
By the same argument,

w(t,x):=\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}R_{n}(u_{0}^{2},f^{2})(t,x)1_{\Omega_{n}}(x)

satsifes that
w(t,\cdot\,)\in L_{1,\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
for each
t\in(0,T]
, and
w\in L_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\big([0,T]\times\Omega\big)
.
In addition, for any
t\in(0,T]
and
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
,

\big\langle w(t,\cdot),\zeta\big\rangle=\langle u_{0}^{2},\zeta\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\big\langle\Delta w(s,\cdot)+f^{2}(s,\cdot),\zeta\big\rangle\,\mathrm{d}s\,.

Put

u:=v-w=\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}R_{n}(u_{0},f)1_{\Omega_{n}}\,.

Then
u(t,\,\cdot\,)\in L_{1,\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)
for each
t\in[0,T]
,
u\in L_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\big([0,T]\times\Omega\big)
, and
u
satisfies (
4.10
).
By applying Fatou’s lemma to (
4.12
) with
(h,H)=(u_{0},f)
, (
4.11
) is obtained.
∎

4.2.
Function spaces for parabolic equations

Throughout this subsection, we assume (
3.16
).
This subsection introduces the function spaces
\Psi B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
,
\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, and
\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.
These spaces correspond to the initial data
u_{0}
, the force term
f
, and the solution
u
for equation (
4.2
), respectively.

For
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and
s\in(0,1]
, by
B_{p}^{n+s}=B_{p}^{n+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
we denote the Besov space whose norm is given by

\displaystyle\|f\|_{B_{p}^{n+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}:=\big\|(1-\Delta)^{n/2}f\big\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\big[(1-\Delta)^{n/2}f\big]_{B_{p}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,,

where
(1-\Delta)^{n/2}f
is introduced in (
3.15
), and

\displaystyle[f]_{B_{p}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}:=\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|f(x+h)-2f(x)+f(x-h)|^{p}}{|h|^{d+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}h\,\,\mathrm{d}x\bigg)^{1/p}\,.

Note that
B_{p}^{n+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
coincides with
B_{p,p}^{n+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
introduced in
[
70
, Definition 2.3.1/2]
, and for any
\gamma,\,s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{B_{p}^{\gamma+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\simeq_{d,p,\gamma,s}\|(1-\Delta)^{s/2}f\|_{B_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

(see,
e.g.
,
[
70
, Theorem 2.3.8/(i), Remark 2.5.12/2]
).
If
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
s\in(0,1)
, then
B_{p}^{n+s}
also coincides with the Sobolev-Slobodeckij space

\displaystyle W_{p}^{n+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):=\,

\displaystyle\Big\{f\in W_{p}^{n}\,:\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|D^{n}f(x)-D^{n}f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{d+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\,\mathrm{d}x<\infty\Big\}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\Big\{f\in W_{p}^{n}\,:\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\{y:|y-x|<1\}}\frac{|D^{n}f(x)-D^{n}f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{d+sp}}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\,\mathrm{d}x<\infty\Big\}

(see,
e.g.
,
[
70
, Theorem 2.5.7/(i)]
).

Let
\zeta_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
,
\widetilde{\rho}
, and
\{\zeta_{0,(n)}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be the functions used in Definition
3.7
(for
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
); recall (
3.17
) - (
3.19
).
Similar to the space
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, we define

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)=\Big\{f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)\,:\,\|f\|^{p}_{B^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}:=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{B^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{p}<\infty\Big\}\,.\end{gathered}

As mentioned in
[
57
, Remark 3.6]
,
B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
has properties similar to
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.
Properties of
B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
are provided in Appendix
A.1
.

For a regular Harnack function
\Psi
, we denote

\displaystyle\Psi B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)=\big\{\Psi g\,:\,g\in B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\big\}\quad\text{and}\quad\|f\|_{\Psi B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}=\|\Psi^{-1}f\|_{B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

The following equaivalent norm on
\Psi B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}
is provided in Proposition
A.8
:
if
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
, then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\Psi B_{p,\theta}^{k+\alpha}}^{p}\simeq_{N}\,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}\int_{\Omega}|\rho^{i}D^{i}f|^{p}\Psi^{-p}\rho^{\theta-d}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.14)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\Big(\int_{y:|y-x|<\frac{\rho(x)}{2}}\frac{|D^{k}f(x)-D^{k}f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{d+\alpha p}}dy\Big)\Psi(x)^{-p}\rho(x)^{(k+\alpha)p+\theta-d}\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

where
N=N(d,p,k,\alpha,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi))
.

Lemma 4.5
.

(1)

Lemmas
3.10
.(1)-(4),
3.12
, and
3.15
hold with
B_{\ast,\ast}^{\ast}(\Omega)
and
B_{\ast}^{\ast}
, instead of
H_{\ast,\ast}^{\ast}(\Omega)
and
H_{\ast}^{\ast}
.

(2)

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
|\gamma|<k
. If
a\in C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{k}(\Omega)
satisfies
|a|^{(0)}_{k}<\infty
, then

\|af\|_{B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\leq N|a|^{(0)}_{k}\|f\|_{B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\theta,k)
.

(3)

If
\gamma^{\prime}>\gamma
, then

\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}+\|f\|_{\Psi B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\leq N\min\big(\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega)},\|f\|_{\Psi B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\big)\,.

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\theta)
.

(4)

If
p\geq 2
, then

\|f\|_{\Psi B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\leq N\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,,

and if
1<p\leq 2
, then

\|f\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\leq N\|f\|_{\Psi B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

Here
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\theta)
.

Lemma
4.5
, except for the counterparts of Lemmas
3.12
and
3.15
(in Lemma
4.5
.(1)), follows from Propositions
A.2
and
A.3
.
The excepted counterparts are proved by repeating the proofs of Lemmas
3.12
and
3.15
with
H_{\ast,\ast}^{\ast}(\Omega)
replaced by
B_{\ast,\ast}^{\ast}(\Omega)
; we left the proof to the reader.

Remark 4.6
.

By repeating the argument of Corollary
3.16
with using the counterpart of Lemma
3.15
in Lemma
4.5
.(1), we obtain that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\Psi B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\simeq_{N}\inf\Big\{\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}\|f_{\alpha}\|_{\Psi B_{p,\theta-|\alpha|p}^{\gamma+n}(\Omega)}:\,f=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}D^{\alpha}f_{\alpha}\Big\}\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\theta,\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi),n)
.

Next, we define function spaces for parabolic equations, following Krylov
[
46
]
.
Let
u
be
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
-valued function on
[0,T]
.

\partial_{t}u
denote a function
f:(0,T)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
satisfying the following condition: for any
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\langle f(\,\cdot\,),\zeta\big\rangle\in L_{1,\mathrm{loc}}\big([0,T]\big)\,\,,\,\,\text{and}\\
&\big\langle u(t),\zeta\big\rangle=\big\langle u(0),\zeta\big\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\big\langle f(s),\zeta\big\rangle\,\,\mathrm{d}s\quad\text{for all}\quad t\in(0,T].\end{split}

(4.15)

In this situation, we also say that
\partial_{t}u=f
in the sense of distribution (on
\Omega
).

Since
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
is a separable topological vector space, if
\partial_{t}u=f
and
\partial_{t}u=g
in the sense of distribution, then
f(s)=g(s)
for almost every
s\in[0,T]
.

We denote

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)=L_{p}\big((0,T);H^{\gamma}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\big)\,\,,\quad

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)=\mathbb{H}_{p}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)\,\,,

(4.16)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)=L_{p}\big((0,T);H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)\big)\,\,,\quad

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)=\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{0}(\Omega,T)\,\,,

\displaystyle\Psi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)=L_{p}\big((0,T);\Psi H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)\big)\,\,,\quad

\displaystyle\Psi\mathbb{L}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)=\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{0}(\Omega,T)\,.

Definition 4.7
.

By
\Psi\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
we denote the space of all functions
u:[0,T]\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
satisfying the following condition:

u\in\Psi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
,
u(0)\in\Psi B^{\gamma+2-2/p}_{p,\theta+2}(\Omega)
, and there exists
\partial_{t}u
in
\Psi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)
.

The norm in
\Psi\mathcal{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
is defined by

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\Psi\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}=\|u\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)}+\|u(0)\|_{\Psi B^{\gamma+2-2/p}_{p,\theta+2}(\Omega)}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega,T)}\,.

For the case
\Psi\equiv 1_{\Omega}
, we denote
\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)=1_{\Omega}\,\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
.

Remark 4.8
.

The initial data space
\Psi B_{p,\theta+2}^{\gamma+2-2/p}(\Omega)
coincides with

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{0}:=\big\{u(0)\,|\,u:[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}

\displaystyle(\Omega)\,\,\,\text{satisfies that}

\displaystyle u\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,\infty)\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\partial_{t}u\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,\infty)\big\}\,.

Note that for
u\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,\infty)
, if there exists
f\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,\infty)
such that

\langle u(t)-u(s),\zeta\rangle=\int_{s}^{t}\langle f(r),\zeta\rangle\,\mathrm{d}r\qquad\forall\,\,0<s<t<\infty\,\,,\,\,\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega),

then
u(0)\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
is (uniquely) well defined to satisfy (
4.15
), by

\displaystyle\langle u(0),\zeta\rangle:=\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\langle u(s),\zeta\rangle-\int_{0}^{s}\langle\partial_{t}u(r),\zeta\rangle\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\,.

The space
\mathrm{Tr}_{0}
is rewritten in the Bochner sense:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{0}=\big\{u(0)\,|\,u:[0,\infty)\rightarrow X_{0}

\displaystyle+X_{1}\,\,\,\text{satisfies that}

\displaystyle u\in L_{p}(\mathbb{R}_{+};X_{0})\,\,,\partial_{t}u\in L_{p}(\mathbb{R}_{+};X_{1})\big\}\,,

where
X_{0}=\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)
,
X_{1}=\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, and
\partial_{t}u
is understood as the weak derivative of
u:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow X_{0}+X_{1}
in the Bochner sense.

It follows from the trace theorem (see,
e.g.
,
[
69
, Theorem 1.8.2]
) and Proposition
A.2
.(5) that

\mathrm{Tr}_{0}=[X_{0},X_{1}]_{1/p,p}=\Psi B_{p,\theta+2}^{\gamma+2-2/p}(\Omega)\,,

where
[X_{0},X_{1}]_{\nu,p}
is the real interpolation space of
X_{0}
and
X_{1}
.
Actually, the second equality is implied by Proposition
A.2
.(5) and that the map
u\mapsto\Psi^{-1}u
is isometric isomorphism from
\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)
(resp.
\Psi H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
,
\Psi B_{p,\theta+2}^{\gamma+2-2/p}(\Omega)
) to
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)
(resp.
H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
,
B_{p,\theta+2}^{\gamma+2-2/p}(\Omega)
).
In addition, we also obtain that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\Psi B_{p,\theta+2}^{\gamma+2-2/p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\simeq_{N}\,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{[X_{0},X_{1}]_{1/p,p}}

\displaystyle\simeq_{p}\,

\displaystyle\inf\Big\{\|u\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}_{+};X_{0})}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L_{p}(\mathbb{R}_{+};X_{1})}\,\,\big|\,\,u:[0,\infty)\rightarrow X_{0}+X_{1}\,\,\,\,\text{satisfies}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\,u\in L_{p}(\mathbb{R}_{+};X_{0})\,\,,\,\,\partial_{t}u\in L_{p}(\mathbb{R}_{+};X_{1})\,\,,\,\,u(0)=f\Big\}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\|u\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,\infty)}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,\infty)}\,\,\big|\,\,u\in\Psi\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,\infty)\,\,,\,\,u(0)=f\right\}\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\theta,\gamma)
.

Proposition 4.9
.

(1)

\Psi\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
is a Banach space.

(2)

C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,\infty)\times\Omega\big)
is dense in
\Psi\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
.

Proof.

The mapping
u\mapsto\Psi^{-1}u
is an isometric isomorphism from
\Psi\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
to
\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega,T)
.
Therefore, we only need to consider the case
\Psi\equiv 1_{\Omega}
.
In this case, (1) and (2) of this proposition are implied by the arguments presented in
[
46
, Remark 5.5]
and
[
48
, Remark 3.8]
, respectively.
We give proofs for the convenience of the reader.

(1) Since
\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
is a normed vector space, we only need to prove the completeness.
By Lemma
3.12
.(2), for any
v\in\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
and
S\in(0,T]
, we have
v-v(0)\in C\big([0,S];H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\big)
with

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,S]}\|v(t)-v(0)\|_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}^{p}\leq N\cdot S^{1-1/p}\|\partial_{t}v\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}\,,

(4.17)

where
N
is independent of
v
and
S
.
Let
\{u^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a Cauchy sequence in
\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
.
Then there exists

\displaystyle(u_{0},f):=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}(u^{n}(0),\partial_{t}u^{n})\quad\text{in}\,\,\,B_{p,\theta+2}^{\gamma+2-2/p}(\Omega)\times\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T).

Moreover, due to (
4.17
), there exists
u:[0,T]\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
such that for any
K\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle u-u_{0}=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\big(u^{n}-u^{n}(0)\big)\quad\text{in}\quad C\big([0,T\wedge K];H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\big)\,.

Therefore, by Lemma
3.12
.(2), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\langle u(t),\zeta\big\rangle=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\big\langle u^{n}(t),\zeta\big\rangle\,&=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\bigg(\langle u_{0}^{n},\zeta\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\big\langle\partial_{t}u^{n}(s),\zeta\big\rangle\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg)\\
&=\langle u_{0},\zeta\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\big\langle f(s),\zeta\big\rangle\,\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
.
Since
u_{n}
is a Cauchy sequence in
\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
, we also obtain that

u=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}u^{n}\quad\text{in}\quad\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)\,.

Consequently,
u\in\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
with
\partial_{t}u=f
and
u(0)=u_{0}
, and
u^{n}\rightarrow u
in
\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
.

(2) In this proof, we use results in Appendix
A.1
.
For
f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
, we denote

\Lambda_{k}f=\sum_{|n|\leq k}\zeta_{0,(n)}f\,.

Due to (
A.33
), for
u\in\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega)
we have

\big\|\Lambda_{k}u-u\big\|_{\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as}\,\,k\rightarrow\infty\,.

Therefore we only need to show that each
\Lambda_{k}u
belongs to the closure of
C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,\infty)\times\Omega\big)
in
\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)
.
By Proposition
A.3
.(8), we obtain that

\Lambda_{k}u\in\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)\,\,,\,\,\,\,\Lambda_{k}\big(\partial_{t}u\big)\in\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)\,\,,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\,\Lambda_{k}u(0)\in B_{p}^{\gamma+2-2/p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\,.

Note that
\partial_{t}\big(\Lambda_{k}u\big)=\Lambda_{k}\big(\partial_{t}u\big)
in the sense of distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
By a standard mollification and cut-off argument, there exist
v_{k,m}\in C_{c}^{\infty}\big([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\big)
such that

\displaystyle I_{k,m}:=\,

\displaystyle\|v_{k,m}-\Lambda_{k}u\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}v_{k,m}-\partial_{t}\Lambda_{k}u\|_{\mathbb{H}_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d},T)}+\|v_{k,m}(0,\cdot)-\Lambda_{k}u(0)\|_{B_{p}^{\gamma+2-2/p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

converges to
0
as
m\rightarrow\infty
.
Put

u_{k,m}=v_{k,m}\sum_{|n|\leq k+1}\zeta_{0,(n)}\,.

Since

\sum_{|n|\leq k+1}\zeta_{0,(n)}\in\bigcap_{l\in\mathbb{N}}C^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\qquad\forall\,\,\,k\in\mathbb{N}\,,

Proposition
A.3
.(8) implies

\displaystyle\|\Lambda_{k}u-u_{k,m}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}=\Big\|(\Lambda_{k}u-v_{k,m})\sum_{|n|\leq k+1}\zeta_{0,(n)}\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma+2}(\Omega,T)}\leq NI_{k,m}\,,

where
N
is independent of
m
.
Since the last term converges to
0
as
m\rightarrow\infty
, the proof is completed.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
\Psi^{\prime}
be a regular Harnack function,
p^{\prime}\in(1,\infty)
and
\gamma^{\prime},\,\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
.
If
f\in\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)\cap\Psi^{\prime}\mathbb{H}_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega,T)
, then for any
\epsilon>0
, there exist
g\in C_{c}^{\infty}((0,T)\times\Omega)
such that

\|f-g\|_{\Psi\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)}+\|f-g\|_{\Psi^{\prime}\mathbb{H}_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega,T)}<\epsilon\,.

Proof.

We denote
X:=\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\cap\Psi^{\prime}H_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega)
, and

\|g\|_{X}:=\|g\|_{\Psi H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}+\|g\|_{\Psi^{\prime}H_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\,.

By a standard molification and cut-off argument, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exist
F\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left((0,T);X\right)
such that
