is the set on the right-hand side of (
6.9
).
Consequently,
\Omega
satisfies
\mathbf{LHMD}(1)
, where
M_{1}
in (
5.6
) depends only on
d
.

This result also implies that the Hardy inequality (
1.3
) holds on
\Omega
, where
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega)
depends only on
d
(see Remark
5.11
).
However, it is worth noting that Marcus, Mizel and Pinchover
[
59
, Theorem 11]
provided that for a convex domain
\Omega
, (
1.3
) holds where
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega)=4
, and
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega)
cannot be chosen less than 4.

Krylov
[
46
]
provided results for the Poisson equation and parabolic equations in
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
.
In this subsection, we extend this result for convex domains; see Corollary
6.11
.
Recall the definitions of
\mathrm{M}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
and
\mathcal{M}_{T}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2})
in the front of Section
4
.

Theorem 6.10
.

Let
\Omega
be a convex domain.
For any
(\alpha^{ij})_{d\times d}\in\bigcup\limits_{0<\nu\leq 1}\mathrm{M}(\nu^{2},1)
,

\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\alpha^{ij}D_{ij}\rho\leq 0

in the sense of distribution.

We temporarily assume Theorem
6.10
and prove Corollary
6.11
.

Corollary 6.11
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be convex,
p\in(1,\infty)
,
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
with

-p-1<\theta-d<-1\,.

(1)

For any
\lambda\geq 0
and
f\in H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
,
the equation

\Delta u-\lambda u=f\,.

has a unique solution
u
in
H^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{H^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}+\lambda\|u\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+2p}(\Omega)}\leq N_{1}\|f\|_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,,

(6.10)

where
N_{1}=N(d,p,\gamma,\theta)
.

(2)

Let
T\in(0,\infty]
and
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{M}_{T}(\nu,\nu^{-1})
for some
\nu\in(0,1]
.
For any
u_{0}\in B^{\gamma+2-2/p}_{p,\theta+2}(\Omega)
and
f\in\mathbb{H}_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega,T)
, the equation

\partial_{t}u=a^{ij}D_{ij}u+f\quad\text{on }\Omega\times(0,T]\quad;\quad u(0,\cdot)=u_{0}

has a unique solution
u
in
\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\gamma+2}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\leq N_{2}\big(\|u_{0}\|_{B^{\gamma+2-2/p}_{p,\theta+2}(\Omega)}+\|f\|_{H_{p,\theta+2p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\big)\,,

(6.11)

where
N_{2}=N(d,p,\theta,\gamma,\nu)
.

In particular,
\Omega
is not necessarily bounded, and
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
are independent of
\Omega
.

Proof of Corollary
6.11
.

Since
\Omega
is convex, (
1.3
) holds on
\Omega
where
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega)=4
.
Put
\Psi=\widetilde{\rho}
which is the regularization of
\rho
in Lemma
3.5
.(1) so that constants
\mathrm{C}_{2}(\widetilde{\rho})
and
\mathrm{C}_{3}(\rho,\widetilde{\rho})
(in Definition
3.1
) can be chosen to depend only on
d
.
It follows from Proposition
4.2
.(2) that for any
\mu\in(-1/p,1-1/p)
,
\mu\in I(\rho,\nu^{2},p)
, and the constant
\mathrm{C}_{4}
in (
4.3
) can be chosen to depend only on
\mu
,
p
and
\nu
.
Putting

\mu=-\frac{\theta-d+2}{p}\in\Big(-\frac{1}{p},1-\frac{1}{p}\,\Big)

and applying Theorems
3.18
and
4.12
, we finish the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.10
.

For
p\in\partial\Omega
, put
W_{p}(x)=(p-x)\cdot e_{p}
, where
e_{p}
is a unit vector satisfying (
6.9
).
We first claim that

\displaystyle\rho(x)=\inf_{p\in\partial\Omega}W_{p}(x)\quad\text{for all}\,\,\,x\in\Omega\,.

(6.12)

For a fixed
x\in\Omega
, we have

\inf_{p\in\partial\Omega}W_{p}(x)=\inf_{p\in\partial\Omega}d(x,F_{p})\geq\rho(x)\,,

where

\displaystyle F_{p}:=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:(y-p)\cdot e_{p}=0\}\subset\Omega^{c}

(6.13)

(see Figure
6.4
above).
For the inverse inequality, take
p_{x}\in\partial\Omega
such that
|x-p_{x}|=\rho(x)
.
Since

B\big(x,\rho(x)\big)\subset\Omega\quad\text{and}\quad p_{x}\in\partial B\big(x,\rho(x)\big)\,,

we obtain that
e_{p_{x}}=(p_{x}-x)/|p_{x}-x|
.
Therefore

\inf_{p\in\partial\Omega}W_{p}(x)\leq W_{p_{x}}(x)=|p_{x}-x|=\rho(x).

Let
\mathrm{A}=(\alpha^{ij})_{d\times d}\in\mathrm{M}(\nu^{2},1)
,
\nu\in(0,1]
, and take
\mathrm{B}\in\mathrm{M}(\nu,1)
such that
\mathrm{B}^{2}=\mathrm{A}
.
For any
p\in\partial\Omega
,

\Delta\big(W_{p}(\mathrm{B}\,\cdot\,)\big)\equiv 0\quad\text{on}\,\,\mathrm{B}^{-1}\Omega\,.

Due to (
6.12
) and Proposition
5.5
.(2), we obtain that
\rho(\mathrm{B}\,\cdot\,)
is a infimum of classical superharmonic functions, and therefore
\rho(\mathrm{B}\,\cdot\,)
is a superharmonic function.
Consequently we have

\langle\alpha^{ij}D_{ij}\rho,\zeta\rangle=\det(\mathrm{A})^{1/2}\big\langle\Delta(\rho(\mathrm{B}\,\cdot\,)),\zeta(\mathrm{B}\,\cdot\,)\big\rangle\leq 0

for any
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
with
\zeta\geq 0
.
∎

6.3.
Exterior Reifenberg condition

The notion of the vanishing Reifenberg condition was introduced by Reifenberg
[
64
]
, and has been extensively studied in the literature (see,
e.g.
,
[
10
,
12
,
31
,
67
]
).
The following definition can be found in
[
10
,
31
]
:
For
\delta\in(0,1)
and
R>0
, a domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is said to satisfy the
(\delta,R)
-
Reifenberg condition
, if for every
p\in\partial\Omega
and
r\in(0,R]
, there exists a unit vector
e_{p,r}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Omega\cap B_{r}(p)\subset\{x\in B_{r}(p)\,:\,(x-p)\cdot e_{p,r}<\delta r\}\quad\text{and}\\
&\Omega\cap B_{r}(p)\supset\{x\in B_{r}(p)\,:\,(x-p)\cdot e_{p,r}>-\delta r\}\,.\end{split}

(6.14)

In addition,
\Omega
is said to satisfy the
vanishing Reifenberg condition
if for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists
R_{\delta}>0
such that
\Omega
satisfies the
(\delta,R_{\delta})
-Reifenberg condition.
Note that the vanishing Reifenberg condition is weaker than the
C^{1}
-boundary condition; see Example
6.14
.(2) and (3).

It was established by Kenig and Toro
[
32
, Lemma 2.1]
that if a bounded domain satisfies the vanishing Reifenberg condition, then this domain also satisfies
\mathbf{LHMD}(1-\epsilon)
for all
\epsilon\in(0,1)
.
Combining this with Corollary
5.13
, we obtain that Statement
5.3

(\Omega,p,\theta)
holds for all
\theta\in(d-p-1,d-1)
.
Furthermore, in addition to the Poisson and heat equations, there have been studies on elliptic and parabolic equations with variable coefficients on domains satisfying the vanishing Reifenberg condition (see,
e.g.
,
[
10
,
11
,
13
,
18
]
)

In this subsection, we present the totally vanishing exterior Reifenberg condition which is a generalization of the Reifenberg condition, and we obtain a result similar to Corollary
6.11
for domains satisfying the totally vanishing exterior Reifenberg condition; see Definition
6.12
and Corollary
6.20
.

Definition 6.12

(Exterior Reifenberg condition)
.

(1)

By
\mathbf{ER}_{\Omega}
we denote the set of all
(\delta,R)\in[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfying the following: for each
p\in\partial\Omega
, and each connected component
\Omega_{p,R}^{(i)}
of
\Omega\cap B(p,R)
, there exists a unit vector
e_{p,R}^{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

\displaystyle\Omega_{p,R}^{(i)}\subset\{x\in B_{R}(p)\,:\,(x-p)\cdot e_{p,R}^{(i)}<\delta R\}\,.

(6.15)

By
\delta(R):=\delta_{\Omega}(R)
we denote the infimum of
\delta
such that
(\delta,R)\in\mathbf{ER}_{\Omega}
.

(2)

For
\delta\in[0,1]
, we say that
\Omega
satisfies the
totally
\delta
-exterior Reifenberg condition
(abbreviate to ‘
\langle\mathrm{TER}_{\delta}\rangle
’), if there exist
0<R_{0}\leq R_{\infty}<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\delta_{\Omega}(R)\leq\delta\quad\text{whenever}\quad R\leq R_{0}\,\,\,\text{or}\,\,\,R\geq R_{\infty}\,.

(6.16)

(3)

We say that
\Omega
satisfies the
totally vanishing exterior Reifenberg condition
(abbreviate to ‘
\langle\mathrm{TVER}\rangle
’), if

\Omega
satisfies the
\delta
-condition for all
\delta\in(0,1]
. In other word,

\lim_{R\rightarrow 0}\delta_{\Omega}(R)=\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\delta_{\Omega}(R)=0\,.

For a comparison between the Refenberg condition and
\langle\mathrm{TVER}\rangle
, see Figure
6.5
and Example
6.14
below.

In this subsection, we provide results on domains satisfying
\langle\mathrm{TER}_{\delta}\rangle
for sufficiently small
\delta>0
.
However, our main interest is thd condition
\langle\mathrm{TVER}\rangle
.

Vanishing
Reifenberg condition
Totally vanishing exterior
Reifenberg condition
(Definition
1.13
)
Totally vanishing exterior
Reifenberg condition
(Definition
6.12
)

Figure 6.5.
Totally vanishing exterior Reifenberg condition

Remark 6.13
.

We claim that for any
R>0
,
\big(\delta(R),R\big)\in\mathbf{ER}_{\Omega}
. Take a sequence
\{\delta_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
(\delta_{n},R)\in\mathbf{ER}_{\Omega}
and
\delta_{n}\rightarrow\delta(R)
.
Let
p\in\partial\Omega
, and let
\Omega^{(i)}
be a connected component of
\Omega\cap B(p,R)
.
There exists a unit vector
e_{n}
such that

\displaystyle\Omega^{(i)}\subset\{x\in B_{R}(p)\,:\,(x-p)\cdot e_{n}<\delta_{n}R\}\,.

(6.17)

Since
\{e_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\partial B(0,1)
, there exists a subsequence
\{e_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
e_{p}:=\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}e_{n_{k}}
exists in
\partial B(0,1)
.
It is impliled by (
6.17
) that

\displaystyle\Omega^{(i)}\subset\{x\in B_{R}(p)\,:\,(x-p)\cdot e_{p}<\delta(R)R\}\,.

Therefore
(\delta(R),R)\in\mathbf{ER}_{\Omega}
.

Example 6.14
.

(1)

If
\Omega
satisfies the
(\delta,R_{1})
-Reifenberg condition,
then
\delta(R)\leq\delta
for all
R\leq R_{1}
, indeed the first line of (
6.14
) implies (
6.15
) with
e_{p,r}^{(i)}=e_{p,r}
.
Moreover, if
\Omega
is bounded, then Propositoin
6.15
implies
\delta(R)\leq\frac{\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)}{R}
.
Therefore, if
\Omega
is a bounded domain satisfying the vanishing Reifenberg condition, then
\Omega
also satisfies
\langle\mathrm{TVER}\rangle
.

(2)

By
\lambda_{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
we denote the little Zygmund class which is the set of all
f\in C(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
such that

\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\frac{|f(x+h)-2f(x)+f(x-h)|}{|h|}=0\,.

For
f\in\lambda_{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
, put

\Omega=\{(x^{\prime},x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}\,:\,x_{d}>f(x^{\prime})\}\,.

Then, as mentioned in
[
12
, Example 1.4.3]
(see also
[
17
, Theorem 6.3]
),
\Omega
satisfies the vanishing Reifenberg condition, which implies
\lim\limits_{R\rightarrow 0}\delta_{\Omega}(R)=0
.
Moreover, since
A:=\|f\|_{C(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}<\infty
, Proposition
6.15
implies that
\delta(R)\leq\frac{2\|f\|_{C(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})}}{R}
.
Therefore
\Omega
satisfies
\langle\mathrm{TVER}\rangle
.

(3)

Suppose that
\Omega
is bounded, and for any
p\in\partial\Omega
there exists
R>0
and
f\in\lambda_{\ast}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
such that

\Omega\cap B(p,R)=\big\{y=(y^{\prime},y_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}\,:\,|y|<R\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,y_{n}>f(y^{\prime})\big\}\,,

where
(y^{\prime},y_{n})=(y_{1},\,\ldots\,,y_{n})
is an orthonormal coordinate system centered at
p
. Then
\Omega
satisfies the vanishing Reifenberg condition, and therefore
\Omega
satisfies
\langle\mathrm{TVER}\rangle
.

(4)

Let
\Omega
satisfy the exterior
R_{0}
-ball condition,
i.e.
, there exists
R_{0}>0
such that for any
p\in\partial\Omega
, there exists
q\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfying
|p-q|=R_{0}
and
B(q,R_{0})\subset\Omega^{c}
.
Then
\delta(R)\leq R/(2R_{0})
, and therefore
\lim\limits_{R\rightarrow 0}\delta(R)=0
.

(5)

If a domain
\Omega
is an intersection of domains satisfying the totally vanishing Reifenberg condition, then
\Omega
satisfies
\langle\mathrm{TVER}\rangle
.

A sufficient condition for
\lim\limits_{R\rightarrow\infty}\delta_{\Omega}(R)=0
is that
\delta_{\Omega}(R)\lesssim 1/R
.
We now provide an equivalent condition for
\Omega
to satisfy
\delta_{\Omega}(R)\lesssim 1/R
.
Note that the definition of
\delta_{\Omega}(R)
implies that
R\delta_{\Omega}(R)
increases as
R\rightarrow\infty
, and therefore if
\delta_{\Omega}(r_{0})>0
for some
r_{0}>0
, then
\delta_{\Omega}(R)\gtrsim 1/R
as
R\rightarrow\infty
.

Proposition 6.15
.

\displaystyle\sup_{R>0}R\,\delta_{\Omega}(R)=\sup_{p\in\partial\Omega}d\big(p,\partial(\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}})\big)\,,

where
\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}}
is the convex hull of
\Omega
, i.e.,

\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}}:=\big\{(1-t)x+ty\,:\,x,\,y\in\Omega\,\,,\,\,t\in[0,1]\big\}\,.

Remark 6.16
.

(1)

\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}}
is an open set, and the smallest convex set containing
\Omega
.

(2)

Proposition
6.15
implies that
\delta_{\Omega}(\,\cdot\,)\equiv 0
if and only if
\Omega
is convex.

Proof of Proposition
6.15
.

We only need to prove that for
0<N_{0}<\infty
,

\displaystyle\sup_{R>0}R\,\delta_{\Omega}(R)\leq N_{0}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\sup_{p\in\partial\Omega}d\big(p,\partial(\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}})\big)\leq N_{0}\,.

(6.18)

Step 1.

We first claim that the LHS of (
6.18
) holds
if and only if for any
p\in\partial\Omega
, there exists a unit vector
e_{p}
such that

\displaystyle\Omega\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,(x-p)\cdot e_{p}<N_{0}\}\,.

(6.19)

The ‘if’ part is obvious, and therefore we only need to prove the ‘only if’ part.
Therefore we assume that the LHS of (
6.18
) holds. Fix
p\in\partial\Omega
, and take
\{\widetilde{\Omega}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfying the following:

(1)

\widetilde{\Omega}_{n}
is a connceted component of
\Omega\cap B_{n}(p)
;

(2)

\widetilde{\Omega}_{1}\subset\widetilde{\Omega}_{2}\subset\widetilde{\Omega}_{3}\subset\cdots\,.

Since
\Omega
is a domain,
\Omega
is path connected, which implies

\displaystyle\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\widetilde{\Omega}_{n}=\Omega\,.

(6.20)

Since
R\delta(R)\leq N_{0}
, for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
e_{n}\in\partial B_{1}(0)
such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Omega}_{n}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,(x-p)\cdot e_{n}<N_{0}\}\,.

(6.21)

Since
\partial B_{1}(0)
is compact, there exists a subsequence
\{e_{n_{k}}\}
such that

\exists\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}e_{n_{k}}=:e_{p}\in\partial B_{1}(0).

Due to (
6.20
) and (
6.21
), we obtain that (
6.19
) holds for this
e_{p}
.

Step 2.

Due to (
6.18
), we only need to prove the following: for
p\in\partial\Omega
,

\text{\eqref{220918300} holds for some $e_{p}\in\partial B_{1}(0)$}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad d\big(p,\partial(\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}})\big)\leq N_{0}\,.

(
\Rightarrow
) Observe that

p\in\partial\Omega\subset\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}}}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,(x-p)\cdot e_{p}\leq N_{0}\}\,.

Put

\alpha_{0}=\sup\{\alpha\geq 0\,:\,p+\alpha e_{p}\in\overline{\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}}}\}\,.

Then
p+\alpha_{0}e_{p}\in\partial(\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}})
, and therefore
d\big(p,\partial(\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}})\big)\leq\alpha_{0}\leq N_{0}
.

(
\Leftarrow
) Take
q\in\partial(\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}})
such that

|p-q|=d\big(p,\partial(\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}})\big)\leq N_{0}\,.

Due to Remarks
6.8
and
6.16
.(1), there exists a unit vector
\widetilde{e}_{q}
such that

\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}}\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,(x-q)\cdot\widetilde{e}_{q}<0\}\,.

This implies that for any
x\in\Omega\subset\Omega_{\mathrm{c.h.}}
,

(x-p)\cdot\widetilde{e}_{q}<(q-p)\cdot\widetilde{e}_{q}\leq|p-q|\leq N_{0}\,.

Therefore (
6.19
) holds for
e_{p}:=\widetilde{e}_{q}
.
∎

Remark 6.17
.

From Step 1 in the proof of Proposition
6.15
, it can be observed that this proposition remains valid even if the definition of
\delta_{\Omega}(R)
is replaced by the infimum of
\delta>0
such that, for any
p\in\partial\Omega
, there exists a unit vector
e_{p,R}
satisfying (
1.15
) with
r=R
.

Now we state the main result of this subsection.
We temporarily assume Theorem
6.18
and Corollary
6.19
(they are proved in the end of this subsection), and prove Corollary
6.20
.

Theorem 6.18
.

For any
\nu\in(0,1]
and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists
\delta_{1}>0
depending only on
d,\,\epsilon,\,\nu
such that if

\Omega
satisfies
\langle\mathrm{TER}_{\delta}\rangle
,
then there exists a measurable function
\phi:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfying the following:

(1)

For any
(\alpha^{ij})_{d\times d}\in\mathrm{M}(\nu^{2},1)
,
\alpha^{ij}D_{ij}\phi\leq 0
in the sense of distribution

(2)

There exists
N=N(d,\nu,\epsilon,R_{0}/R_{\infty})>0
such that

N^{-1}\rho(x)^{1-\epsilon}\leq\phi(x)\leq N\rho(x)^{1-\epsilon}\quad\text{for all}\,\,\,x\in\Omega\,,

where
R_{0}
and
R_{\infty}
are constants in (
6.16
).

Corollary 6.19
.

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists
\delta_{2}>0
depending only on
d,\,\epsilon
such that if

\Omega
satisfies
\langle\mathrm{TER}_{\delta}\rangle
,
then
\Omega
satisfies
\mathbf{LHMD}(1-\epsilon)
.
Moreover,
M_{1-\epsilon}
in (
5.6
) depends only on
d,\,\epsilon
and
R_{0}/R_{\infty}
, where
R_{0}
and
R_{\infty}
are constants in (
6.16
).

Corollary 6.20
.

Let
p\in(1,\infty)
,
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
,
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
,
\nu\in(0,1]
with

-p-1<\theta-d<-1\,.

Then there exists
\delta>0
depending only on
d,p,\epsilon,\nu
such that if

\Omega
satisfies
\langle\mathrm{TER}_{\delta}\rangle
,
then the assertions of (1) and (2) in Corollary
6.11
hold for this
\Omega
, where
N_{0}
in (
6.10
) depends only on
d
,
p
,
\gamma
,
\theta
,
R_{0}/R_{\infty}
, and
N_{1}
in (
6.11
) depends only on
d
,
p
,
\gamma
,
\theta
,
\nu
,
R_{0}/R_{\infty}
.
Here,
R_{0}
and
R_{\infty}
are constants in (
6.16
).

Proof of Corollary
6.20
.

Take
\epsilon\in(0,1)
such that

-p-1+(p-1)\epsilon<\theta-d<-1-\epsilon

and put

\mu=-\frac{\theta-d+2}{p(1-\epsilon)}\in\big(-\frac{1}{p},1-\frac{1}{p}\big)\,.

Put
\delta=\delta_{1}\wedge\delta_{2}>0
, where
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
are constants in Theorem
6.18
and Corollary
6.19
, respectively, for given
\epsilon
and
\nu
.

By Corollary
6.19
and Remark
5.11
, the Hardy inequality (
1.3
) holds on
\Omega
where
\mathrm{C}_{0}(\Omega)
depends only on
d,\epsilon,R_{\infty}/R_{0}
.
Let
\phi
be the function in Theorem
6.18
.
Due to Proposition
4.2
.(2), we obtain that
\mu\in I(\phi,\nu^{2},p)
and
\mathrm{C}_{4}
in (
4.3
) can be chosen to depend only on
\mu
,
\nu
, and
p
.
Put
\Psi=\widetilde{\rho}^{\,1-\epsilon}
which is a regularization of
\phi
.
Then
\mathrm{C}_{2}(\Psi)
and
\mathrm{C}_{3}(\phi,\Psi)
can be chosen to depend only on
d
,
\epsilon
,
\nu
and
R_{0}/R_{\infty}
.
By applying Theorem
3.18
and Theorem
4.12
, the proof is completed.
∎

To prove Theorem
6.18
, we need to construct functions used instead of the harmonic measure.

Lemma 6.21
.

Suppose that
(\delta,R)\in\mathbf{ER}_{\Omega}
.
For any
\nu\in(0,1)
and
p\in\partial\Omega
, there exists a continuous function
w_{p,R}:\Omega\rightarrow(0,1]
satisfying the following:

(1)

For any
\mathrm{B}\in\mathrm{M}(\nu,1)
,
w_{p,R}(\mathrm{B}\,\cdot\,)
is a classical superharmonic function on
\mathrm{B}^{-1}\Omega
.

(2)

w_{p,R}=1\text{ on }\{x\in\Omega\,:\,|x-p|>(1-\delta)R\}
.

(3)

w_{p,R}\leq M\delta
on
\Omega\cap B(p,\delta R)
.

Here,
M
depends only on
\nu
and
d
.
In particular,
M
is independent of
\delta
.

Proof of Lemma
6.21
.

If
\delta>1/8
, then by putting
w_{p,R}\equiv 1
and
M=8
, this lemma is proved.
Therefore we only need to consider the case
\delta\leq 1/8
.
For a fixed
p\in\partial\Omega
, let
\big\{\Omega^{(i)}_{p,R}\big\}
be the set of all connected components of
\Omega\cap B(p,R)
.
For each
i
, take a unit vector
e_{p,R}^{(i)}
satisfying (
6.15
).
Put

\displaystyle q=p+R(\delta+1/4)e^{(i)}_{p,R}

(6.22)

so that

\displaystyle|p-q|=R(\delta+1/4)\quad\text{and}\quad\Omega_{p,R}^{(i)}\cap B(q,R/4)\neq\emptyset

(6.23)

(see Figure
6.6
below).

\Omega_{p,R}^{(i)}
p
q
e_{p,R}^{(i)}

Figure 6.6.
q
and
B(q,R/4)
in (
6.22
), (
6.23
)

Put

\displaystyle W^{(i)}(x)=\frac{1-(4R^{-1}|x-q|)^{2-\nu^{-2}d}}{1-2^{2-\nu^{-2}d}}\,.

(6.24)

Then we have

\sum_{k,l}\alpha^{kl}D_{kl}\,W^{(i)}\leq 0\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\,\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{q\},\quad\text{for all }(\alpha^{kl})_{d\times d}\in\mathrm{M}(\nu^{2},1)\,.

Indeed, for
f\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, if
f^{\prime}\geq 0
and
f^{\prime\prime}\leq 0
then

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{k,l=1}^{d}\alpha^{kl}D_{kl}\big(f(|x|)\big)\\
=\,\,&\frac{\sum_{i,j}\alpha^{kl}x_{k}x_{l}}{|x|^{2}}f^{\prime\prime}(|x|)+\left(\frac{\sum_{k}\alpha^{kk}}{|x|}-\frac{\sum_{k,l}\alpha^{kl}x_{k}x_{l}}{|x|^{3}}\right)f^{\prime}(|x|)\\
\leq\,\,&\nu^{2}f^{\prime\prime}(|x|)+\frac{d-\nu^{2}}{|x|}f^{\prime}(|x|)\,.\end{split}

(6.25)

Observe that

\displaystyle 0\leq W^{(i)}(x)\,

\displaystyle\leq M_{0}\big(4R^{-1}|x-q|-1\big)\quad

\displaystyle\text{if}\,\,\,|x-q|\geq R/4\,;

\displaystyle W^{(i)}(x)\,

\displaystyle\geq 1\quad

\displaystyle\text{if}\,\,\,|x-q|\geq R/2\,,

where
M_{0}
is a constant depends only on
\nu
and
d
.
Due to (
6.23
) and that
\delta<\frac{1}{8}
, for
x\in\Omega_{p,R}^{(i)}
,

\displaystyle\text{if\quad$|x-p|\leq\delta R$}\,\,,\,\,\,

\displaystyle\text{then\quad$\frac{R}{4}\leq|x-q|\leq\frac{R}{4}+2\delta R$}\,;

\displaystyle\text{if\quad$|x-p|\geq(1-\delta)R$}\,\,,\,\,\,

\displaystyle\text{then\quad$|x-q|\geq\frac{(3-8\delta)R}{4}\geq\frac{R}{2}$}\,.

Therefore we obtain that

\displaystyle\quad 0\leq

\displaystyle W^{(i)}(x)\leq 8M_{0}\delta\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if}\,\,\,|x-p|\leq\delta R

\displaystyle W^{(i)}(x)\geq 1\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if}\,\,\,|x-p|\geq(1-\delta)R\,.

Put

\displaystyle w_{p,R}(x)=\begin{cases}W^{(i)}(x)\wedge 1&\text{if}\,\,\,x\in\Omega^{(i)}_{p,R}\\
1&\text{if}\,\,\,x\in\Omega\setminus B(p,R)\,.\end{cases}

Then
w_{p,R}
is continuous on
\Omega
, and satisfies (2) and (3) of this lemma.
(1) of this lemma follows from (
6.24
) and Proposition
5.5
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.18
.

We only need to prove for
\nu\in(0,1)
.
Let
M>0
be the constant in Lemma
6.21
.
For a fixed
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, take small enough
\delta\in(0,1)
such that
M\delta<\delta^{1-\epsilon}
, and take small enough
\eta\in(0,1)
such that

(1-\eta)M\delta+\eta\leq\delta^{1-\epsilon}\,.

We assume that
\Omega
satisfies (
6.16
) for this
\delta
.
By using dilation and Remark
6.13
, without lose of generality, we assume that
(\delta,R)\in\mathbf{ER}_{\Omega}
whenever
R\leq\widetilde{R}_{0}:=R_{0}/R_{\infty}\,\,(\leq 1)
or
R\geq 1
.

Step 1.

Put

k_{0}=\min\big\{k\in\mathbb{N}\,:\,\delta^{k}\leq\widetilde{R}_{0}\big\}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{I}=\{k\in\mathbb{Z}\,:\,k\leq 0\,\,\,\text{or}\,\,\,k\geq k_{0}\}\,,

so that
(\delta,\delta^{k})\in\mathbf{ER}_{\Omega}
for every
k\in\mathcal{I}
.
For each
p\in\partial\Omega
and
k\in\mathcal{I}
, put

\displaystyle\phi_{p,k}=\delta^{k(1-\epsilon)}\Big((1-\eta)\,w_{p,\delta^{k}}+\eta\Big)\,,

where
w_{p,\delta^{k}}
is the function
w_{p,R}
in Lemma
6.21
with
R=\delta^{k}
.
Note that

\displaystyle\qquad\phi_{p,k}(x)\leq\delta^{(k+1)(1-\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\text{on}\quad\Omega\cap\overline{B}(p,\delta^{k+1})\,;

\displaystyle\qquad\phi_{p,k}(x)=\delta^{k(1-\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\text{on}\quad\Omega\cap\partial B(p,\delta^{k})\,;

\displaystyle\eta\cdot\delta^{k(1-\epsilon)}\leq\phi_{p,k}\leq\delta^{k(1-\epsilon)}\qquad

\displaystyle\text{on}\quad\Omega\cap B(p,\delta^{k})\,.

Put

\displaystyle\phi_{p}^{(1)}(x):=\inf\{\phi_{p,k}(x)\,:\,k\geq k_{0}\,\,,\,\,|x-p|<\delta^{k}\}\quad

\displaystyle\text{for}\quad|x-p|<\delta^{k_{0}}\,;

\displaystyle\phi_{p}^{(2)}(x):=\inf\{\phi_{p,k}(x)\,:\,k\leq 0\,\,,\,\,|x-p|<\delta^{k}\}\quad

\displaystyle\text{for}\quad|x-p|>\delta\,.

The similar argument with the proof of Theorem
5.12
implies that for any
\mathrm{B}\in\mathrm{M}(\nu,1)
,
\phi_{p}^{(1)}(\mathrm{B}\,\cdot\,)
and
\phi_{p}^{(2)}(\mathrm{B}\,\cdot\,)
are classical superharmonic functions on

\{\mathrm{B}^{-1}x\,:\,x\in\Omega\cap B(p,\delta^{k})\}\quad\text{and}\quad\{\mathrm{B}^{-1}x\,:\,x\in\Omega\setminus\overline{B}(p,\delta)\}\,,

respectively.
Moreover, for each
i\in\{1,2\}
,
\phi_{p}^{(i)}(x)
satisfies

\displaystyle\eta|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}\leq\phi_{p}^{(i)}(x)\leq\delta^{-1+\epsilon}|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}

(6.26)

on its domain.

Step 2.

Observe that

\displaystyle\phi_{p}^{(1)}(x)\leq\phi_{p,k_{0}}(x)\leq\delta^{(k_{0}+1)(1-\epsilon)}\quad

\displaystyle\text{if}\,\,\,|x-p|=\delta^{k_{0}+1}\,\,;

(6.27)

\displaystyle\phi_{p}^{(1)}(x)=\phi_{p,k_{0}}(x)=\delta^{k_{0}(1-\epsilon)}\quad

\displaystyle\text{if}\,\,\,|x-p|=\delta^{k_{0}}\,.

Put
\gamma=-\nu^{-2}d+2<0
and take
\alpha_{1},\,\beta_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
f(t):=\alpha_{1}-\beta_{1}t^{\gamma}
satisfies

\displaystyle f(\delta^{k_{0}+1})=\delta^{(k_{0}+1)(1-\epsilon)}\quad\text{and}\quad f(\delta^{k_{0}})=\delta^{k_{0}(1-\epsilon)}\,.

(6.28)

Since
f(\delta^{k_{0}+1})<f(\delta^{k_{0}})
, we have
\beta_{1}>0
, which implies the following:

•

Due to (
6.25
), for any
(\alpha^{ij})_{d\times d}\in\mathrm{M}(\nu^{2},1)
,

\sum_{i,\,j}\alpha^{ij}D_{ij}\Big(f\big(|\cdot-p|\big)\Big)\leq 0\,;

•

f(t)
increases as
t\rightarrow\infty
.
In particular,
f(t)\geq\delta^{(k_{0}+1)(1-\epsilon)}
for all
t\geq\delta^{k_{0}+1}
.

Put

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(1)}:=\begin{cases}\phi_{p}^{(1)}&\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\big\{x\in\Omega\,:\,|x-p|\leq\delta^{k_{0}+1}\big\}\\
\phi_{p}^{(1)}\wedge f(|\,\cdot\,-p|)&\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\big\{x\in\Omega\,:\,\delta^{k_{0}+1}<|x-p|<\delta^{k_{0}}\big\}\\
f(|\,\cdot\,-p|)&\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\big\{x\in\Omega\,:\,|x-p|\geq\delta^{k_{0}}\big\}\,.\end{cases}

Due to Proposition
5.5
.(4), (
6.27
), and (
6.28
), we obtain that for any
\mathrm{B}\in\mathrm{M}(\nu,1)
,
\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(1)}(\mathrm{B}\,\cdot\,)
is a classical superharmonic function on
\mathrm{B}^{-1}\Omega
.

Take
\alpha_{2}>0,\,\beta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}\eta\delta^{1-\epsilon}+\beta_{2}=f(\delta)\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha_{2}\delta^{-1+\epsilon}+\beta_{2}=f(1)\,.

(6.29)

Then, due to (
6.26
) and (
6.29
),
\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(2)}:=\alpha_{2}\phi_{p}^{(2)}+\beta_{2}
satisfies that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(1)}(x)=f(\delta)\leq\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(2)}(x)\quad

\displaystyle\text{on}\,\,\,\{x\in\Omega\,:\,|x-p|=\delta\}\quad(\text{in the sense of limit});

(6.30)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(1)}(x)=f(1)\geq\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(2)}(x)\quad

\displaystyle\text{on}\,\,\,\{x\in\Omega\,:\,|x-p|=1\}

(see (
6.26
)).
Due to Proposition
5.5
.(4), (
6.30
), and that
\alpha_{2}>0
, the function

\displaystyle\phi_{p}(x):=\begin{cases}\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(1)}&\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\big\{x\in\Omega\,:\,|x-p|\leq\delta\big\}\,;\\
\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(1)}\wedge\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(2)}&\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\big\{x\in\Omega\,:\,\delta<|x-p|<1\big\}\,;\\
\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(2)}&\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\big\{x\in\Omega\,:\,|x-p|\geq 1\big\}\,,\end{cases}

satisfies that for any
\mathrm{B}\in\mathrm{M}(\nu,1)
,

\phi_{p}(\mathrm{B}\,\cdot\,)
is a classical superharmonic function on
\mathrm{B}^{-1}\Omega
.

(6.31)

Step 3.

We claim that for every
x\in\Omega
,

\displaystyle N^{-1}|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}\leq\phi_{p}(x)\leq N|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}\,,

(6.32)

where
N=N(d,\epsilon,\nu,\widetilde{R}_{1})>0
.
Note that

\displaystyle\phi_{p}=\begin{cases}\vskip 2.84526pt\,\,\,\phi_{p}^{(1)}&\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\big\{x\in\Omega\,:\,|x-p|\leq\delta^{k_{0}+1}\big\}\\
\vskip 2.84526pt\,\,\,\phi_{p}^{(1)}\wedge f(|\,\cdot\,-p|)&\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\big\{x\in\Omega\,:\,\delta^{k_{0}+1}<|x-p|<\delta^{k_{0}}\big\}\\
\vskip 2.84526pt\,\,\,f(|\,\cdot\,-p|)&\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\big\{x\in\Omega\,:\,\delta^{k_{0}}\leq|x-p|\leq\delta\big\}\\
\vskip 2.84526pt\,\,\,f(|\,\cdot\,-p|)\wedge\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(2)}&\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\big\{x\in\Omega\,:\,\delta<|x-p|<1\big\}\\
\vskip 2.84526pt\,\,\,\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(2)}&\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\big\{x\in\Omega\,:\,|x-p|\geq 1\big\}\,.\end{cases}

(6.33)

Step 3.1)
It is provided in (
6.26
) that

\displaystyle\eta|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}\leq\phi_{p}^{(1)}(x)\leq\delta^{-1+\epsilon}|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}\quad\text{on}\quad\big\{x\in\Omega\,:\,|x-p|<\delta^{k_{0}}\big\}\,.

(6.34)

Step 3.2)
Since

f(t)=\alpha_{1}-\beta_{1}t^{\gamma}\,\,,\,\,\,\,\beta_{1}>0\,\,,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\,\gamma<-d+2\leq 0\,,

we have

\displaystyle\delta^{(k_{0}+1)(1-\epsilon)}=f(\delta^{k_{0}+1})\leq f\big(|x-p|\big)\leq f(1)\quad\text{if}\quad\delta^{k_{0}+1}<|x-p|<1

(6.35)

(this implies that
f(1)=\alpha_{1}-\beta_{1}>0
).

Step 3.3)
Note that
\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(2)}=\alpha_{2}\phi_{p}^{(2)}+\beta_{2}
and
\alpha_{2}>0
.
Take
K\geq 1
such that

\alpha_{2}\eta K^{1-\epsilon}\geq 2|\beta_{2}|\,.

For
x\in\Omega
satisfying
\delta<|x-p|<K
, it follows from (
6.26
), (
6.29
), and (
6.35
) that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(2)}(x)\geq\alpha_{2}\eta\delta^{1-\epsilon}+\beta_{2}=f(\delta)\geq\delta^{(k_{0}+1)(1-\epsilon)}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(2)}(x)\leq\alpha_{2}\delta^{-1+\epsilon}K^{1-\epsilon}+\beta_{2}\,.

Therefore there exists
N=N(\delta,\alpha_{2},\beta_{2},K)
such that

\displaystyle N^{-1}|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}\leq\widetilde{\phi}^{(2)}_{p}(x)\leq N|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}\,.

(6.36)

Step 3.4)
If
|x-p|\geq K
, then

2|\beta_{2}|\leq\alpha_{2}\eta K^{1-\epsilon}\leq\alpha_{2}\eta|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}\,.

Due to (
6.26
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\eta\alpha_{2}}{2}|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}\leq\widetilde{\phi}_{p}^{(2)}(x)\leq\alpha_{2}\big(\delta^{-1+\epsilon}+\frac{\eta}{2}\,\big)|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}\,.

(6.37)

Since
k_{0},\eta,\,\alpha_{1}\,\beta_{1},\,\alpha_{2},\,\beta_{2},\,K
depend only on
d,\,\nu,\,\epsilon,\,\delta,\,\widetilde{R}_{0}
,
(
6.33
) - (
6.37
) imply (
6.32
).

Step 4.

Put
\phi(x):=\inf\limits_{p\in\partial\Omega}\phi_{p}(x)
.
Then

N^{-1}\rho(x)\leq\phi(x)\leq N\rho(x)\,,

where
N
is the same constant as in (
6.32
).
For any fixed
\mathrm{B}\in\mathrm{M}(\nu,1)
, due to (
6.31
) and Proposition
5.5
.(2),
\phi(\mathrm{B}\,\cdot\,)
is superharmonic on
\mathrm{B}^{-1}\Omega
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
6.19
.

For a given
\epsilon>0
, let
\delta
be the constant in Theorem
6.18
with
\nu=1/2
, and supposes that (
6.16
) holds for this
\delta
.
The proof of Theorem
6.18
(see (
6.31
) and (
6.32
)) implies that for each
p\in\partial\Omega
, there exists a classical superharmonic function
\phi_{p}
such that

N_{0}^{-1}|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}\leq\phi_{p}(x)\leq N_{0}|x-p|^{1-\epsilon}\quad\text{for any}\,\,\,x\in\Omega\,,

where
N_{0}=N(d,\epsilon,R_{\infty}/R_{0})>0
.
Note that

N_{0}r^{-1+\epsilon}\phi_{p}\geq 1\quad\text{on}\quad\Omega\cap\partial B_{r}(p)\,.

From the definition of the harmonic measure
w(\,\cdot\,,p,r)
(see (
5.5
)), we obtain that if
r>0
and
x\in\Omega\cap B_{r}(p)
, then

w(x,p,r)\leq N_{0}r^{-1+\epsilon}\phi_{p}(x)\leq N_{0}^{2}\Big(\frac{|x-p|}{r}\Big)^{1-\epsilon}\,.

Therefore we obtain (
5.6
) with
\alpha=1-\epsilon
and
M_{\alpha}=N_{0}^{2}
.
∎

6.4.
Conic domains

\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
denotes the set
\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,|x|=1\}
, and
A_{\mathbb{S}}
denotes the surface measure on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.
Note that for any nonnegative Borel function
F
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}F(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}F(r\sigma)\,\mathrm{d}A_{\mathbb{S}}(\sigma)\Big)r^{d-1}\,\mathrm{d}r\,.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a relatively open set of
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, and define

\displaystyle\Omega=\big\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\,:\,\frac{x}{|x|}\in\mathcal{M}\big\}

which is the conic domain generated by
\mathcal{M}
(see Figure
6.7
below).

Figure 6.7.
Conic domains

We denote

B_{R}^{\Omega}=\Omega\cap B_{R}(0)\quad\text{and}\quad Q_{R}^{\Omega}=(1-R^{2},1]\times B_{R}^{\Omega}\,.

In this subsection, we suppose that
\mathcal{M}
satisfies Assumption
6.23
; this assumption is satisfied if
\mathcal{M}
is a Lipschitz domain in
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.
We prove that if
u
satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{cases}u_{t}=\Delta u\quad&\text{in}\quad Q_{1}^{\Omega}\,;\\
u=0\quad&\text{on}\quad(0,1]\times\big((\partial\Omega)\cap B_{1}(0)\big)\,,\end{cases}

then for any
\lambda\in(0,\lambda_{0})
and
0<R<1
,

\displaystyle|u(t,x)|\lesssim_{\mathcal{M},\lambda,r}|x|^{\lambda}\sup_{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}|u|\quad\text{whenever}\quad(t,x)\in Q_{R}^{\Omega}

(6.38)

(see Remark
6.26
), where
\lambda_{0}
is the constant defined in (
6.47
).

Remark 6.22
.

As shown in
[
42
]
, estimate (
6.38
) is closely related to Heat kernel estimates.
In
[
42
, Lemma 3.9]
, Kozlov and Nazarov used the type of estimate (
6.38
) to obtain estimates for the kernel
G
of parabolic equations in
C^{1,1}
-cones.

Before state the main result of this subsection, Theorem
6.25
, we introduce spherical gradient and spherical Laplacian, avoding notions of differential geometry.
For a function
f
on
\mathcal{M}
, we denote
F_{f}(x)=f(x/|x|)
.
We denote

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle=\text{the set of all $f:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}$ for which $F_{f}\in C^{\infty}(\Omega)$}\,;

\displaystyle C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle=\{f\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})\,:\,\mathrm{supp}(f)\subset\mathcal{M}\}\,.

The spherical gradient and spherical Laplacian of
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
, denoted by
\nabla_{\mathbb{S}}f
and
\Delta_{\mathbb{S}}f
, are defined by

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbb{S}}f=\nabla F_{f}|_{\mathcal{M}}\quad\text{and}\quad\Delta_{\mathbb{S}}f=\Delta F_{f}|_{\mathcal{M}}\,.

(6.39)

A direct calculation gives the following:

•

For any
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
and
g\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}}\big(\nabla_{\mathbb{S}}f,\nabla_{\mathbb{S}}g\big)_{d}\,\mathrm{d}A_{\mathbb{S}}=-\int_{\mathcal{M}}(\Delta_{\mathbb{S}}f)\cdot g\,\,\mathrm{d}A_{\mathbb{S}}\,,

where
(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{d}
is the inner product on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

•

For any
F\in C^{\infty}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle|\nabla F|^{2}=|D_{r}F|^{2}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}|\nabla_{\mathbb{S}}F|^{2}\,.

(6.40)

•

For a function
F\in C^{\infty}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\Delta F=D_{rr}F+\frac{d-1}{r}D_{r}F+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Delta_{\mathbb{S}}F\,.

(6.41)

In (
6.40
) and (
6.41
),
F
is also considered a function on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{M}
defined as
(r,\sigma)\mapsto F(r\sigma)
.
We leave it to the reader to verify that
\nabla_{\mathbb{S}}
(resp.
\Delta_{\mathbb{S}}
) is equivalent with the gradient (resp. Laplace-Beltrami) operator implied by standard differential structure on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
; see
[
30
]
for the standard differential structure on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.

We make certain assumption about
\mathcal{M}
to applicate the results in Subsections
3
and
4
.

Assumption 6.23
.

We denote
\partial_{\mathbb{S}}\mathcal{M}=\overline{\mathcal{M}}\setminus\mathcal{M}
.

(1)

\mathcal{M}
is a connected (relatively) open set of
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with
\overline{\mathcal{M}}\neq\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.

(2)

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in\partial_{\mathbb{S}}\mathcal{M}\\
r\in(0,1]\end{subarray}}\frac{A_{\mathbb{S}}\big(\{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\setminus\mathcal{M}\,:\,|\sigma-p|<r\}\big)}{r^{d-1}}>0\,,

(6.42)

(3)

Let
w_{0}(\sigma)
be the first (positive) Dirichlet eigenfunction of the spherical Laplacian
\Delta_{\mathbb{S}}
on
\mathcal{M}
(see Proposition
6.24
.(1)).
There exist constants
A,\,N>0
such that

\displaystyle w_{0}(\sigma)\geq N^{-1}d(\sigma,\partial_{\mathbb{S}}\mathcal{M})^{A}\,.

(6.43)

By
\mathring{W}_{2}^{1}(\mathcal{M})
, we denotes the closure of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})
in

W_{2}^{1}(\mathcal{M}):=\{f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathcal{M})\,:\,\|f\|_{L_{2}(\mathcal{M})}+\|\nabla_{\mathbb{S}}f\|_{L_{2}(\mathcal{M})}<\infty\}\,.

Proposition 6.24
.

(1)

If Assumption
6.23
.(1) holds, then

\displaystyle\Lambda_{0}:=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})\\
w\not\equiv 0\end{subarray}}\frac{\int_{\mathcal{M}}|\nabla_{\mathbb{S}}w|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}A_{\mathbb{S}}}{\int_{\mathcal{M}}|w|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}A_{\mathbb{S}}}>0\,,

(6.44)

and there exists a unique
w_{0}
in
C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})\cap\mathring{W}_{2}^{1}(\mathcal{M})
such that

\displaystyle w_{0}>0\quad,\quad\int_{\mathcal{M}}|w_{0}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}A_{\mathbb{S}}=1\quad,\quad\Delta_{\mathbb{S}}w_{0}+\Lambda_{0}w_{0}=0\,.

(6.45)

Moreover,
w_{0}
is bounded on
\mathcal{M}
. Furthermore, the function

\displaystyle W_{0}(x):=|x|^{\lambda_{0}}w_{0}(x/|x|)

(6.46)

is a positive harmonic function on
\Omega
, where

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}=-\frac{d-2}{2}+\sqrt{\Lambda_{0}+\Big(\frac{d-2}{2}\Big)^{2}}>0\,.

(6.47)

(2)

If Assumption
6.23
.(2) holds, then

\displaystyle\inf_{p\in\partial\Omega,\,r>0}\frac{m\big(\Omega^{c}\cap B_{r}(p)\big)}{r^{d}}>0\,.

(3)

Let
-e_{d}\notin\overline{\mathcal{M}}
and define
\phi_{d}
to be the stereographic projection from
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\setminus\{-e_{d}\}
to
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
given by

\displaystyle\phi_{d}(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{d-1},\sigma_{d})=\Big(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{1+\sigma_{d}},\ldots,\frac{\sigma_{d-1}}{1+\sigma_{d}}\Big)\,.

(6.48)

If
\phi_{d}(\mathcal{M})
is a John domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
(see Remark
3.6
for the definition of a John domain), then Assumption
6.23
.(3) holds.

Proof.

(1) (
6.44
) follows from
[
24
, Theorems 10.13, 10.18, 10.22]
.
It is provided in
[
24
, Theorem 10.11, Corollary 10.12]
that there exists a unique
w_{0}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})\cap\mathring{W}_{2}^{1}(\mathcal{M})
satisfying (
6.45
).

To prove the boundedness of
w_{0}
, without lose of generality, we assume that
-e_{d}:=(0,\ldots,0,-1)\notin\mathcal{M}
.
By
\phi_{d}
we denote the stereographic projection from
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\setminus\{-e_{d}\}
to
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
defined by (
6.48
).
Then
\phi_{d}(\mathcal{M})
is a bounded domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
.
Consider the function
\widetilde{w}_{0}:\phi_{d}(\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined as
\widetilde{w}_{0}(\phi_{d}(x)):=w_{0}(x)
.
Then
\widetilde{w}_{0}
belongs to
\mathring{W}_{2}^{1}\big(\phi_{d}(\mathcal{M})\big)
and satisfies

\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}a^{ij}D_{ij}\widetilde{w}_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}b^{i}D_{i}\widetilde{w}_{0}+\Lambda_{0}\widetilde{w}_{0}=0\quad\text{in}\quad\phi_{d}(\mathcal{M})\subset\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\,.

Here,
a^{ij},\,b^{i}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})
(
i,\,j=1,\,\ldots,\,d
) are smooth functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
such that there exists
\nu>0
satisfying

\nu|\xi|^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}a^{ij}(x)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\leq\nu^{-1}|\xi|^{2}\qquad\forall\,\,\xi=(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\,\,,\,\,x\in\phi_{d}(\mathcal{M})\,.

The boundedness of
\widetilde{w}_{0}
follows from classical results for elliptic equations (see,
e.g.
,
[
52
, Theorem 3.13.1]
), and this implies that
w_{0}
is bounded.

It directly follows from (
6.41
) that the function
W_{0}
in (
6.46
) is harmonic on
\Omega
.

(2)
For any
p\in\partial_{\mathbb{S}}\mathcal{M}
and
r\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\big\{s\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,s\in(1-r/2,1+r/2),\,\sigma\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\cap B_{r/2}(p)\big\}

\displaystyle\subset

\displaystyle\,\,B_{r}(p)

\displaystyle\subset

\displaystyle\,\big\{s\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,s\in(1-r,1+r),\,\sigma\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\cap B_{2r}(p)\big\}\,.

Therfore (
6.42
) holds if and only if

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in\partial_{\mathbb{S}}\mathcal{M}\\
r\in(0,1]\end{subarray}}\frac{m\big(\Omega^{c}\cap B_{r}(p)\big)}{r^{d}}>0\,,

(6.49)

where
m
is the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Ir
r\geq 2
, then
B_{r}(p)\supset B_{r/2}(0)
.
Therefore

\displaystyle\inf_{p\in\partial_{\mathbb{S}}\mathcal{M},\,r\geq 2}\frac{m\big(\Omega^{c}\cap B_{r}(p)\big)}{r^{d}}\geq\inf_{r\geq 1}\frac{m\big(\Omega^{c}\cap B_{r}(0)\big)}{(2r)^{d}}=\frac{A_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\setminus\mathcal{M}\right)}{2^{d}d}>0\,.

(6.50)

Consequently, it is implied by (
6.49
) and (
6.50
) that

\displaystyle\inf_{p\in\partial\Omega,\,r>0}\frac{m\big(\Omega\cap B_{r}(p)\big)}{r^{d}}>0\,.

(3) We denote
U_{\mathcal{M}}:=\phi_{d}(\mathcal{M})
.
It follows from Example
3.2
.(2) that
W_{0}
(in (
6.46
)) is a Harnack function on
\Omega
.
Since
W_{0}
is a Harnack function, and
\phi_{d}
(resp.
\phi_{d}^{-1}
) is Lipschitz continuous on
\mathcal{M}
(resp.
U_{\mathcal{M}}
), we obtain that
\widetilde{w}_{0}:=w_{0}\circ\phi_{d}^{-1}
is a Harnack on
U_{\mathcal{M}}
(see Lemma
3.3
).
In addition,
d(\sigma,\partial_{\mathbb{S}}\mathcal{M})\simeq d\big(\phi_{d}(\sigma),\partial U_{\mathcal{M}}\big)
.
By Remark
3.6
, if
\Omega
is a John domain, then

\widetilde{w}_{0}(x^{\prime})\gtrsim d(x^{\prime},\partial U_{\mathcal{M}})^{A}\qquad\text{for all}\quad x^{\prime}\in\phi_{d}(\mathcal{M})\,,

and therefore (
6.43
) is proved.
∎

Theorem 6.25
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\subset\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\,(d\geq 2)
satisfy Assuption
6.23
, and suppose that
u\in C^{\infty}(Q_{1}^{\Omega})
satisfies that

\displaystyle u_{t}=\Delta u\quad\text{in}\quad Q_{1}^{\Omega}\,;

\displaystyle\lim_{(t,x)\rightarrow(t_{0},x_{0})}u(t,x)=0\quad\text{whenever}\quad 0<t_{0}\leq 1\,\,,\,\,x_{0}\in(\partial\Omega)\cap B_{1}.

Then for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and
R\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle|u(t,x)|\leq N\Big(\sup_{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}|u|\Big)W_{0}(x)^{1-\epsilon}\qquad\forall\,\,(t,x)\in Q_{R}^{\Omega}\,,

where
W_{0}
is the function defined in (
6.46
) and
N=N(\mathcal{M},\epsilon,R)>0
.

Recall that
\mathring{W}_{2}^{1}\big(B_{1}^{\Omega}\big)
is the closure of
C_{c}^{\infty}\big(B_{1}^{\Omega}\big)
in
W_{2}^{1}\big(B_{1}^{\Omega}\big)
.

Remark 6.26
.

Theorem
6.25
implies that if
u
satisfies the assumptions in Theorem
6.25
and
\lambda\in(0,\lambda_{0})
, where
\lambda_{0}
is in (
6.47
), then

\displaystyle|u(t,x)|\leq N\Big(\sup_{Q_{1}^{\mathcal{D}}}|u|\Big)|x|^{\lambda}\quad\text{on}\,\,\,Q_{R}^{\Omega}

(6.51)

where
N=N(\mathcal{M},\lambda,R)
.
We note that for
\lambda>\lambda_{0}
, (
6.51
) does not hold in general.
Observe that
u(t,x):=W_{0}(x)
satisfies assumptions in Theorem
6.25
.
Due to (
6.46
), there is no constant
N
satisfying (
6.51
) with
u(t,x)=W_{0}(x)
and
\lambda>\lambda_{0}
.

Proof of Theorem
6.25
.

Step 1.

Put
K=A\vee\lambda_{0}
where
A
and
\lambda_{0}
are the constants in (
6.43
) and (
6.47
), respectively.
From direct calculation (see,
e.g.
,
[
38
, Lemma 3.4.(1)]
) we obtain that

\displaystyle d(\sigma,\partial\Omega)\leq d(\sigma,\partial_{\mathbb{S}}\mathcal{M})\leq 2d(\sigma,\partial\Omega)\quad\text{for all}\quad\sigma\in\mathcal{M}\,.

Therefore for
x\in\Omega\cap B_{1}(0)
, we have

\rho(x)^{K}=d(x,\partial\Omega)^{K}\simeq|x|^{K}d\big(x/|x|,\partial_{\mathbb{S}}\mathcal{M}\big)^{K}\leq|x|^{\lambda_{0}}d(x/|x|,\partial_{\mathbb{S}}\mathcal{M})^{A}\lesssim W_{0}(x)\,.

Due to Proposition
6.24
.(2) and Remark
5.11
,
\Omega
satisfies
\mathbf{LHMD}(\alpha)
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
Take small enough
\delta\in(0,1)
such that

\big((d+2)^{-1}+\alpha^{-1}\big)\delta<\epsilon\quad\text{and}\quad 1-\frac{d+4}{d+2}\delta>0\,,

and put

\beta_{t}=\frac{\delta}{d+2}\quad\text{and}\quad\beta_{x}=1-\delta-2\beta_{t}\,.

Then
\epsilon
,
\delta
,
\beta_{t}
,
\beta_{x}
satisfy (
5.31
).
Put

\epsilon_{i}=\epsilon+\frac{\beta_{x}}{K}i\quad\text{for}\quad i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,\,,\,\,\,\,\text{and}\qquad i_{0}=\Big[\frac{1-\epsilon}{\beta_{x}}K\Big]

such that
\epsilon_{i_{0}}\leq 1<\epsilon_{i_{0}+1}
.
Since
W_{0}
is bounded on
B_{1}^{\Omega}
(see Proposition
6.24
), we have

\sup_{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}|W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i_{0}+1}}u|\lesssim_{\Omega,\epsilon}\sup_{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}|u|\,,

and therefore we only need to prove that for any
i\in\{0,1,\ldots,i_{0}\}
and
0<R_{1}<R_{2}\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{R_{1}}^{\Omega}}|W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i}}u|\lesssim N(\mathcal{D},\epsilon,R,r)\sup_{Q_{R_{2}}^{\Omega}}|W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i+1}}u|\,.

(6.52)

Step 2.

Take
\eta_{0}\in C^{\infty}\big(\mathbb{R}\big)
such that
\eta_{0}(s)=1
if
s<R_{1}^{2}
and
\eta_{0}(s)=0
if
s>R_{2}^{2}
, and put
\eta(t,x):=\eta_{0}(1-t)\eta_{0}\big(|x|^{2}\big)
. Note that

\displaystyle\eta(t,x)=\begin{cases}\vskip 1.42262pt1\quad&\text{if}\quad t>1-R_{1}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad|x|<R_{1}\,;\\
0\quad&\text{if}\quad t<1-R_{2}^{2}\quad\text{or}\quad|x|>R_{2}\,.\end{cases}

Put

\displaystyle v=u\eta\,\,,\,\,\,\,f^{0}:=\big(\partial_{t}\eta+\Delta\eta\big)u\,\,,\,\,\,\,f^{i}:=-2uD_{i}\eta\quad(i=1,\,\ldots,\,d)\,,

(6.53)

so that
v\in C\big(\overline{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}\big)\cap C^{\infty}(Q_{1}^{\Omega})
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v=\Delta v+f_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}f^{i}\quad\text{in}\quad Q_{1}^{\Omega}\quad;\quad v\equiv 0\quad\text{on}\quad\overline{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}\setminus Q_{1}^{\Omega}\,.

Step 2.1)
We first claim that
v\in\mathcal{H}_{2,d-2}^{1}(\Omega,1)
.
Since

\displaystyle\Big\|f^{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}f^{i}\Big\|_{\mathbb{H}_{2,d+2}^{-1}(B_{1}^{\Omega},1)}\lesssim\left\|f^{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2,d+2}(B_{1}^{\Omega},1)}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left\|f^{i}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2,d}(B_{1}^{\Omega},1)}\lesssim\sup_{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}|u|

(see Lemmas
3.8
and
3.12
),
there exists
\widetilde{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{2,d-2}^{1}(\Omega,1)
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\widetilde{v}=\Delta\widetilde{v}+f^{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}f^{i}\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{v}(0\,\cdot\,)=0\,.

For the claim in this step, we only need to prove that

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}\in C^{\infty}(\Omega)\cap C\big(\overline{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}\big)\,\,,\,\,\text{and}\quad\widetilde{v}\equiv 0\quad\text{on}\quad\overline{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}\setminus Q_{1}^{\Omega}\,.

(6.54)

Indeed, if (
6.54
) holds, then the maximum principle yields that
v\equiv\widetilde{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{2,d-2}^{1}(B_{1}^{\Omega},1)
.
Since
\mathrm{supp}\big(v(t,\cdot)\big)\subset\overline{B_{R_{2}}^{\Omega}}
for each
t\in[0,1]
,
v
belongs to
\mathcal{H}_{2,d-2}^{1}(\Omega,1)
.

Let us prove (
6.54
).
Since
f^{0},f^{i}\in C^{\infty}(Q_{1}^{\Omega})
, we obtain that
\widetilde{v}\in C^{\infty}(Q_{1}^{\Omega})
.
Note that
B_{1}^{\Omega}
satisfies
LHMD
(
\alpha^{\prime}
) for some
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,1)
(see Proposition
6.24
.(2) and Remark
5.11
), and therefore there exists a superharmonic function
\psi
such that
\psi\simeq(\rho_{B_{1}^{\Omega}})^{\alpha^{\prime}/2}
(see Theorem
5.12
), where
\rho_{B_{1}^{\Omega}}(x):=d(x,\partial B_{1}^{\Omega})
.
Take
\beta_{x}^{\prime},\,\beta_{t}^{\prime},\,\delta^{\prime},\,\epsilon^{\prime}>0
such that (
5.31
) holds (for
\alpha^{\prime}
instead of
\alpha
), and
2\beta_{t}^{\prime}+\delta^{\prime}<1/2
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\Big\||\psi^{-1+\epsilon^{\prime}}(\rho_{B_{1}^{\Omega}})^{2-2\beta_{t}^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}}f^{0}|+\sum_{i=1}^{d}|\psi^{-1+\epsilon^{\prime}}(\rho_{B_{1}^{\Omega}})^{1-2\beta_{t}^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}}f^{i}|\Big\|_{L_{(d+2)/\delta}(Q_{1}^{\Omega},\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,\,

\displaystyle\Big\||(\rho_{B_{1}^{\Omega}})^{(3-\alpha^{\prime})/2}f^{0}|+\sum_{i=1}^{d}|(\rho_{B_{1}^{\Omega}})^{(1-\alpha^{\prime})/2}f^{i}|\Big\|_{L_{(d+2)/\delta}(Q_{1}^{\Omega},\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\,\,

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}|u|<\infty\,.

Therefore, Theorem
5.18
and Remark
5.20
yield that

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}t^{-\beta_{t}^{\prime}}(\rho_{B_{1}^{\Omega}})^{-(1-\epsilon^{\prime})\alpha^{\prime}/2}|\widetilde{v}|\lesssim\sup_{0<t\leq 1}\frac{\big|\widetilde{\psi}^{-1+\epsilon^{\prime}}\big(\widetilde{v}(t,\cdot)-\widetilde{v}(0,\cdot)\big)\big|_{\beta_{x}^{\prime}}^{(0)}}{|t-0|^{\beta_{t}^{\prime}}}<\infty

(6.55)

(for the first inequality, see Proposition
3.17
).
Since
\widetilde{v}\in C^{\infty}(Q_{1}^{\Omega})
, (
6.55
) implies that
\widetilde{v}\in C\big(\overline{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}\big)
and
\widetilde{v}\equiv 0
on
\overline{Q_{1}^{\Omega}}\setminus Q_{1}^{\Omega}
.
Therefore (
6.54
) is proved.

Step 2.2)

To prove (
6.52
), assume that the left hand side of (
6.52
) is finite.

Recall that
\Omega
admits the Hardy inequality (see Proposition
6.24
.(2) and Remark
5.11
),
v\in\mathcal{H}_{2,d-2}^{1}(\Omega,1)
(in (
6.53
)) is a solution of the equation

\partial_{t}v=\Delta v+f_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}f^{i}\quad;\quad v(0,\cdot)\equiv 0

(see Step 2.1), and that
W_{0}
is a regular Harnack function (see Example
3.2
.(2)).
Since

\displaystyle\Big\||W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i}}\rho^{\beta_{x}+1}f^{0}|+|W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i}}\rho^{\beta_{x}}f^{i}|\Big\|_{L_{(d+2)/\delta}((0,1]\times\mathcal{D};\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}x)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\,

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{R_{2}}^{\Omega}}\big|W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i}}\rho^{\beta_{x}}u\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\,

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{R_{2}}^{\Omega}}\big|W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i}+\beta_{x}/K}u\big|=\sup_{Q_{R_{2}}^{\Omega}}\big|W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i+1}}u\big|

(where
N=N(r,R_{1},R_{2},\mathcal{M})
), Theorem
5.18
(with Remark
5.20
) implies that
v\in W_{0}^{1-\epsilon_{i}}\mathcal{H}_{p,-2-2\beta_{t}p}^{1}(\Omega,1)
and

\displaystyle\sup_{(0,1]\times\Omega}t^{-\beta_{t}}\big|W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i}}v|

(6.56)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{R_{1}}\,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(0,1]}\frac{\big|W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i}}v(t,\cdot)-W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i}}v(0,\cdot)\big|_{\beta_{x}}^{(0)}}{|t-0|^{\beta_{t}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\,

\displaystyle\Big\||W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i}}\rho^{\beta_{x}+1}f^{0}|+|W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i}}\rho^{\beta_{x}}f^{i}|\Big\|_{L_{(d+2)/\delta}((1-R_{2}^{2},1]\times\Omega;\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}x)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\,

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{R_{2}}^{\Omega}}\big|W_{0}^{-1+\epsilon_{i+1}}u\big|\,.

Since
v\equiv u
in
Q_{R_{1}}^{\Omega}
, (
6.56
) implies (
6.52
).
∎

Appendix A
Weighted Sobolev/Besov spaces

A.1.
Weighted Sobolev/Besov spaces without regular Harnack functions

The spaces
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
were initially developed for studying partial differential equations in domains, as demonstrated in
[
37
,
46
,
57
]
.
Moreover, these spaces, along with similar function spaces like
B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, have also been found in studies on Fourier multipliers arising in harmonic analysis, as seen in works such as
[
14
,
23
,
53
]
.

In this subsection, we introduce the properties of the spaces
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
and
B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, which are independent of the previous contents of this paper, except for Subsection
3.1
which is used only for specifying
\widetilde{\rho}
satisfying (
A.4
).
The contents of this subsection are based on the properties of
H_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
B_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

In this section, we assume that

d\in\mathbb{N}\,\,,\,\,\,\,p\in(1,\infty)\,\,,\,\,\,\,\gamma,\,\theta\in\mathbb{R}\,\,,\,\,\,\,\text{$\Omega$ is an open set in $\mathbb{R}^{d}$}\,,

and denote

\mathcal{I}=\{d,\,p,\,\gamma,\,\theta\}\,.

By
X_{p}^{\gamma}
and
X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, we denote either
H_{p}^{\gamma}\,(\,=H_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, or
B_{p}^{\gamma}\,(\,=B_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and
B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.

The spaces
H_{p}^{\gamma}
and
B_{p}^{\gamma}
are introduced in Subsections
3.2
and
4.2
, respectively.
Recall the following elementary properties of
X_{p}^{\gamma}
, which can be found in
[
70
, Corollary 2.8.2, Theorem 2.10.2]
:

\displaystyle\|af\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}\lesssim_{d,p,\gamma}\|a\|_{C^{[|\gamma|]+1}}\|f\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|f(A,\cdot)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}\lesssim_{d,p,\gamma,A}\|f\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}\,.

(A.1)

We also recall the definitions of
X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.
Fix
\zeta_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}\geq 0\,\,,\,\,\,\ \text{supp}(\zeta_{0})\subset[e^{-1},e]\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\zeta_{0}(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\equiv 1\quad\text{on}\,\,\mathbb{R}_{+}\,.

(A.2)

Put
\zeta_{1}(t)=\zeta_{0}(e^{-1}t)+\zeta_{0}(t)+\zeta_{0}(et)
, so that

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}\cdot\zeta_{0}\equiv\zeta_{0}\quad\text{on}\quad\mathbb{R}_{+}\,.

(A.3)

For
\xi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, we denote

\xi_{(n)}(x)=\xi(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(x))\,,

where
\widetilde{\rho}(x)
is the regularization of
\rho(x)
constructed in Lemma
3.5
.(1).
Note the following properties of
\widetilde{\rho}
and
\xi_{n}(n)
:

•

For each
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exists
N_{k}=N_{k}(d,k)>0
such that

\displaystyle N_{0}\rho(x)\leq\widetilde{\rho}(x)\leq N_{0}\rho(x)\quad\text{and}\quad|D^{k}\widetilde{\rho}(x)|\leq N_{k}\widetilde{\rho}(x)^{-k+1}

(A.4)

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
x\in\Omega
.

•

Let
\xi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
be supported on
[e^{-K},e^{K}]\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
K\in\mathbb{N}
.
For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\begin{gathered}\text{supp}(\xi_{(n)})\subset\{x\in\Omega\,:\,e^{n-K}\leq\widetilde{\rho}(x)\leq e^{n+K}\}\,;\\
\xi_{(n)}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\quad\text{with}\quad|D^{\alpha}\xi_{(n)}|\leq N(\alpha,\xi)\,e^{-n|\alpha|}\,.\end{gathered}

(A.5)

In addition, since
\sum_{|n|\leq K+1}\zeta_{0}(e^{n}\cdot)\equiv 1
on
[e^{-K},e^{K}]
, we have

\displaystyle\xi_{(n)}\equiv\xi_{(n)}\sum_{|k|\leq N}\zeta_{0,{(n+k)}}\,.

We denote

\displaystyle X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\Big\{f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)\,:\,\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}^{p}:=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\big(\zeta_{(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}<\infty\Big\}\,,

\displaystyle l^{\theta/p}_{p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})

\displaystyle=\Big\{\{f_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subset X_{p}^{\gamma}\,:\,\|\{f_{n}\}\|_{l^{\theta/p}_{p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})}^{p}:=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|f_{n}\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}<\infty\Big\}\,.

For
\xi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
, we define the maps

\displaystyle S_{\xi}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}:=\big\{\{f_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\,:\,f_{n}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\big\}\,;

\displaystyle R_{\xi}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)

as

\displaystyle S_{\xi}f

\displaystyle:=\big\{(S_{\xi}f)_{n}\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}:=\big\{\big(f\xi_{(n)}\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\,,

\displaystyle R_{\xi}\{f_{n}\}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\xi_{(n)}(\,\cdot\,)f_{n}(e^{-n}\,\cdot\,)\,.

Note that, since
\zeta_{1}\zeta_{0}\equiv\zeta_{0}
,
R_{\zeta_{1}}\circ S_{\zeta_{0}}
is the identity map on
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
.
Following
[
57
]
, we use the maps
S_{\xi}
and
R_{\xi}
to obtain properties of
X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
from the properties of
l_{p}^{\theta/p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})
.

We now introduce the properties of
X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.
Since
\zeta_{0}
is fixed and the spaces
X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
are independent of choice of
\zeta_{0}
(see Proposition
A.3
.(5)), the dependence on
\zeta_{0}
will be ignored.
For the case
X=H
, Propositions
A.1
-
A.3
follow from
[
57
, Section 2, 3]
and elementary properies of
H_{p}^{\gamma}
.
Corresponding results for the case
X=B
can also be obtained in a similar way.
However, it needs to be clearly stated in
[
57
]
that the constants in the inequalities in Propositions
A.1
-
A.3
are independent of
\Omega
.
Therefore we provide proof of these propositions to verify the case
X=B
and to investigate the dependence of the constants in each inequality.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
\xi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
.
For any
f\in X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
and
\{f_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\in X_{p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
,

\|S_{\xi}f\|_{l_{p}^{\theta/p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})}\leq N\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\quad\text{and}\quad\|R_{\xi}\{f_{n}\}\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\leq N\|\{f_{n}\}\|_{l_{p}^{\theta/p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})}\,,

(A.6)

where
N=N(\mathcal{I},\zeta,\xi)
.

Proof.

Take
K\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathrm{supp}(\xi)\subset\left[e^{-K},e^{K}\right]
so that

\displaystyle|n-k|>K\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\zeta_{0,(n)}\xi_{(k)}\equiv 0\,.

(A.7)

Due to (
A.2
) and (
A.7
), we have

\displaystyle\xi_{(n)}=\sum_{|k|\leq K}\xi_{(n)}\zeta_{0,(n+k)}\,.

(A.8)

From (
A.8
), (
A.5
), and (
A.1
), we have

\displaystyle\|S_{\xi}f\|_{l^{\theta/p}_{p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})}^{p}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\big(\xi_{(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{K,p}

\displaystyle\sum_{|k|\leq K}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\xi_{(n)}(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\big(\zeta_{0,(n+k)}f\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{I},\xi}

\displaystyle\sum_{|k|\leq K}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\big(\zeta_{0,(n+k)}f\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{I},K}

\displaystyle\sum_{|k|\leq K}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\big(\zeta_{0,(n+k)}f\big)(e^{n+k}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{K|\theta|}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}\,.

Therefore the first inequality in (
A.6
) is proved.

Due to (
A.8
), (
A.5
), and (
A.1
), we have

\displaystyle\|R_{\xi}\{f_{n}\}\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|\zeta_{0,(n)}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\xi_{(k)}(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)f_{k}(e^{n-k}\,\cdot\,)\Big\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{K,p}

\displaystyle\sum_{|k|\leq K}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|\zeta_{0,(n)}(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\xi_{(n+k)}(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)f_{n+k}(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\Big\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{I},\zeta,\xi,K}

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\|f_{n+k}(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{I},K}

\displaystyle\|\{f_{n}\}\|_{l_{p}^{\theta/p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})}^{p}\,.

Therefore the second inequality in (
A.6
) is proved.
∎

Proposition A.2

(Properties of weighted Sobolev/Besov spaces - I)
.

(1)

X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}
is a Banach space.

(2)

C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
is dense in
X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.

(3)

X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
is the dual of
X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)
, where

\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}=1\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\theta}{p}+\frac{\theta^{\prime}}{p^{\prime}}=d\,.

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega),\,g\neq 0}\frac{\big|(f,g)\big|}{\|g\|_{X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)}}\simeq_{\mathcal{I}}\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

(A.9)

In particular,
X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
is reflexive.

(4)

Let
p_{i}\in(1,\infty)
and
\gamma_{i}
,
\theta_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
for
i=0,\,1
.
For any
t\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\big[X^{\gamma_{0}}_{p_{0},\theta_{0}}(\Omega),X^{\gamma_{1}}_{p_{1},\theta_{1}}(\Omega)\big]_{t}\simeq_{N}X^{\gamma_{t}}_{p_{t},\theta_{t}}(\Omega)

where
N=N(d,p_{i},\theta_{i},\gamma_{i},t;i=1,2)
.
Here,
[Y_{0},Y_{1}]_{t}
is the complex interpolation space of
Y_{0}
and
Y_{1}
(see
[
69
, Section 1.9]
for the definition and properties of the complex interpolation spaces), and
p_{t}\in(1,\infty)
and
\gamma_{t},\,\theta_{t}\in\mathbb{R}
are constants satisfying

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{t}}=\frac{1-t}{p_{0}}+\frac{t}{p_{1}}\,\,,\,\,\,\,\gamma_{t}=(1-t)\gamma_{0}+t\gamma_{1}\,\,\,,\,\,\,\,\frac{\theta_{t}}{p_{t}}=(1-t)\frac{\theta_{0}}{p_{0}}+t\frac{\theta_{1}}{p_{1}}\,.

(A.10)

(5)

Let
p_{i}\in(1,\infty)
and
\gamma_{i}
,
\theta_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
for
i=0,\,1
, with
\gamma_{0}\neq\gamma_{1}
.
For any
t\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\big(H^{\gamma_{0}}_{p_{0},\theta_{0}}(\Omega),H^{\gamma_{1}}_{p_{1},\theta_{1}}(\Omega)\big)_{t,p_{t}}\simeq_{N}B^{\gamma_{t}}_{p_{t},\theta_{t}}(\Omega)\simeq_{N}\big(B^{\gamma_{0}}_{p_{0},\theta_{0}}(\Omega),B^{\gamma_{1}}_{p_{1},\theta_{1}}(\Omega)\big)_{t,p_{t}}

where
N=N(d,p_{i},\theta_{i},\gamma_{i},t;i=1,2)
.
Here,
(Y_{0},Y_{1})_{t,p_{t}}
is the real interpolation space of
Y_{0}
and
Y_{1}
(see
[
69
, Section 1.3]
for the definition and properties of the real interpolation spaces), and
p_{t}\in(1,\infty)
and
\gamma_{t},\,\theta_{t}\in\mathbb{R}
are constants satisfying (
A.10
).

Proof.

(1) We only need to prove that if
\{f^{(n)}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, then this sequence converges in
X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.
Due to (
A.6
),
S_{\zeta_{0}}f^{(n)}
is a Cauchy sequence in
l_{p}^{\theta/p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})
, and therefore there exists
\lim\limits_{n\rightarrow\infty}S_{\zeta_{0}}f^{(n)}=:F
in
l_{p}^{\theta/p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})
.
Put
f=R_{\zeta_{1}}F\in X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, so that

\|f-f^{(n)}\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}=\|R_{\zeta_{1}}\big(F-S_{\zeta_{0}}f^{(n)}\big)\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|F-S_{\zeta_{0}}f^{(n)}\|_{l_{p}^{\theta/p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})}\rightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
.
The proof is completed.

(2) If
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
, then
\|\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}=0
for all but finitely many
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Therefore
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)\subset X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.
To prove that
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
is dense in
X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, note that
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is dense in
X_{p}^{\gamma}
.
For any
f\in X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
and
\epsilon>0
, since
S_{\zeta_{0}}{f}\in l_{p}^{\theta/p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})
, there exists
\{g_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
g_{n}\equiv 0
for all but finitely many
n
, and

\big\|S_{\zeta_{0}}f-\{g_{n}\}\big\|_{l_{p}^{\theta/p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})}<\epsilon\,.

Since
g_{n}\equiv 0
for all but finitely many
n
,
g:=R_{\zeta_{1}}\{g_{n}\}
belongs to
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
.
Due to

f-g=R_{\zeta_{1}}\big(S_{\zeta_{0}}f-\{g_{n}\}\big)

and (
A.6
), we obtain

\|f-g\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\leq N\big\|S_{\zeta_{0}}f-\{g_{n}\}\big\|_{l_{p}^{\theta/p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})}\leq N\epsilon\,,

where
N=N(\mathcal{I})
. Since
N
is independent of
\epsilon
, the proof is completed.

(3) Observe that for any
g\in X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)
and
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\langle g,f\rangle|\,&\leq\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big|\big\langle g,\zeta_{0,(n)}f\big\rangle\big|=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big|\big\langle\zeta_{1,(n)}g,\zeta_{0,(n)}f\big\rangle\big|\\
&=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big|\big\langle(e^{nd}S_{\zeta_{1}}g)_{n},(S_{\zeta_{0}}f)_{n}\big\rangle\big|\\
&\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}}\|S_{\zeta_{1}}g\|_{l^{-\theta/p+d}_{p^{\prime}}(X_{p^{\prime}}^{-\gamma})}\|S_{\zeta_{0}}f\|_{l^{\theta/p}_{p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})}\\
&\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}}\|g\|_{X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)}\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.\end{split}

(A.11)

For
g\in X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)
, let
\mathbf{L}_{g}
be the linear map from
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
to
\mathbb{R}
defined by

\mathbf{L}_{g}f=\langle g,f\rangle\,.

Then (
A.11
) and (1) of this proposition imply that
\mathbf{L}_{g}\in\big(X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\big)^{\ast}
with

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{L}_{g}\|_{\left(X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\right)^{\ast}}=\sup_{f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega),\,f\neq 0}\frac{|\langle g,f\rangle|}{\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}}\|g\|_{X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

In other words,
\mathbf{L}:g\mapsto\mathbf{L}_{g}
is a bounded linear operator from
X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)
to
\big(X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\big)^{\ast}
.
We claim that
\mathbf{L}
is bijective and for any
g\in X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\|g\|_{X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}}\|\mathbf{L}_{g}\|_{\left(X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\right)^{\ast}}\,.

(A.12)

- Injectivity :
If
\mathbf{L}_{g}\equiv 0
, then
\mathbf{L}_{g}f=\langle g,f\rangle=0
for all
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
.
Therefore
g
is the zero distribution.

- Surjectivity :
For
\Lambda\in\big(X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\big)^{\ast}
,
\Lambda R_{\zeta_{1}}
is in
\big(l^{\theta/p}_{p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})\big)^{\ast}\simeq l^{-\theta/p}_{p^{\prime}}(X_{p^{\prime}}^{-\gamma})
(see,
e.g.
,
[
70
, Theorem 2.11.2]
).
Therefore there exists
\{\widetilde{g}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in l^{-\theta/p}_{p^{\prime}}(X_{p^{\prime}}^{-\gamma})
such that

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\Lambda R_{\zeta_{1}}\{f_{n}\}=\sum_{n}\langle\widetilde{g}_{n},f_{n}\rangle\quad\text{for all $\{f_{n}\}\in l_{p}^{\theta/p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})$}\,,\,\,\text{and}\\
\big\|\{\widetilde{g}_{n}\}\big\|_{l^{-\theta/p}_{p^{\prime}}(X_{p^{\prime}}^{-\gamma})}\simeq_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|\Lambda R_{\zeta_{1}}\big\|_{\left(l^{\theta/p}_{p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})\right)^{\ast}}\,.\end{gathered}

(A.13)

For any
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Lambda f\,&=\Lambda\big(R_{\zeta_{1}}S_{\zeta_{0}}f\big)=\big(\Lambda R_{\zeta_{1}}\big)\big(S_{\zeta_{0}}f\big)\\
&=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\big\langle\widetilde{g}_{n},(S_{\zeta_{0}}f)_{n}\big\rangle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}e^{-nd}\big\langle\widetilde{g}_{n}(e^{-n}\,\cdot\,)\zeta_{0,(n)},f\big\rangle\\
&=\big\langle R_{\zeta_{0}}\{e^{-nd}\widetilde{g}_{n}\},f\big\rangle\,.\end{split}

(A.14)

Since

\|\{e^{-nd}\widetilde{g}_{n}\}\|_{l_{p^{\prime}}^{\theta^{\prime}/p^{\prime}}(X_{p^{\prime}}^{-\gamma})}=\|\{\widetilde{g}_{n}\}\|_{l_{p}^{-\theta/p}(X_{p^{\prime}}^{-\gamma})}<\infty\,,

we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{g}:=R_{\zeta_{0}}\{e^{-nd}\widetilde{g}_{n}\}\in X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)\,,

(A.15)

Consequently, (
A.14
) and (
A.15
) yield
\Lambda=\mathbf{L}_{\widetilde{g}}
, and teh surjectivity is proved.

- (
A.12
) :
Let
g\in X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)
.
For
\Lambda:=\mathbf{L}_{g}
, we recall
\{\widetilde{g}_{n}\}
and
\widetilde{g}:=R_{\zeta_{0}}\{e^{-nd}\widetilde{g}_{n}\}
in (
A.13
) - (
A.15
).
Since
\mathbf{L}
is bijective,
\widetilde{g}=g
.
It is implied by (
A.6
), (
A.13
) - (
A.15
) that

\displaystyle\|g\|_{X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|\{\widetilde{g}_{n}\}\|_{l_{p^{\prime}}^{-\theta/p}(X_{p^{\prime}}^{-\gamma})}\simeq_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|\Lambda R_{\zeta_{1}}\big\|_{\left(l^{\theta/p}_{p}(X_{p}^{\gamma})\right)^{\ast}}\lesssim\|\Lambda\|_{\left(X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\right)^{\ast}}\,.

Although we have only proved

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega),\,f\neq 0}\frac{|\langle g,f\rangle|}{\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}}\|g\|_{X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)}

(A.16)

for
g\in X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)
, the proofs of (
A.11
) and (
A.12
) imply that (
A.16
) holds for all
g\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)
.

The reflexivity of
X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
follows from that
\big(X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\big)^{\ast\ast}\simeq\big(X_{p^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}^{-\gamma}(\Omega)\big)^{\ast}\simeq X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
.

(4) Although the formula for
\theta
in (
A.10
) is different within
[
57
, Proposition 2.4]
, the formula in (
A.10
) is sufficient for our purpose.
Indeed, this proposition is implied by Proposition
A.1
,
[
69
, Theorem 1.2.4]
, and that

\displaystyle\big[l^{\theta_{0}/p_{0}}_{p_{0}}(X^{\gamma_{0}}_{p_{0}}),l^{\theta_{1}/p_{1}}_{p_{1}}(X^{\gamma_{1}}_{p_{1}})\big]_{t}\simeq_{N}l^{\theta_{t}/p_{t}}_{p_{t}}(X^{\gamma_{t}}_{p_{t}})\,,

where
N=N(d,p_{i},\gamma_{i},t;i=1,2)
(see,
e.g.
,
[
69
, Theorem 1.18.1, Theorem 2.4.2/1]
).

(5) This proposition is implied by Proposition
A.1
,
[
69
, Theorem 1.2.4]
, and that

\displaystyle\big(l^{\theta_{0}/p_{0}}_{p_{0}}(H^{\gamma_{0}}_{p_{0}}),l^{\theta_{1}/p_{1}}_{p_{1}}(H^{\gamma_{1}}_{p_{1}})\big)_{t,p_{t}}\simeq_{N}l^{\theta_{t}/p_{t}}_{p_{t}}(B^{\gamma}_{p_{t}})

(see,
e.g.
,
[
69
, Theorem 1.18.1, Theorem 2.4.2/1.(a)]
).
∎

Proposition A.3

(Properties of weighted Sobolev/Besov spaces - II)
.

(1)

If
p\geq 2
, then

\|f\|_{B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}}\|f\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,,

and if
1<p\leq 2
, then

\|f\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}}\|f\|_{B_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

(2)

For any
s<\gamma
,

\|f\|_{H_{p,\theta}^{s}(\Omega)}+\|f\|_{B_{p,\theta}^{s}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I},s}\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

(3)

(Sobolev embedding) Let
p_{i}\in(1,\infty)
and
\gamma_{i}
,
\theta_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
for
i=0,\,1
, with that

\gamma_{0}>\gamma_{1}\,\,,\quad\gamma_{0}-\frac{d}{p_{0}}=\gamma_{1}-\frac{d}{p_{1}}\,\,,\quad\frac{\theta_{0}}{p_{0}}=\frac{\theta_{1}}{p_{1}}\,.

Then

\|f\|_{X_{p_{1},\theta_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}(\Omega)}+\|f\|_{B_{p_{1},\theta_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}(\Omega)}\leq N\|f\|_{X_{p_{0},\theta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0}}(\Omega)}

where
N=N(d,p_{i},\gamma_{i},\theta_{i};i=1,2)
.

(4)

(Pointwise multiplier) For
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, let
a\in C^{k}_{loc}(\Omega)
satisfy

\displaystyle|a|_{k}^{(0)}:=\sup_{\Omega}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq k}\rho^{|\alpha|}|D^{\alpha}a|<\infty\,\,.

If
|\gamma|\leq k
then

\displaystyle\|af\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}}|a|_{k}^{(0)}\|f\|_{H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\,,

(A.17)

for all
f\in H^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)
, and if
|\gamma|<k
then

\|af\|_{B^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{\mathcal{I}}|a|_{k}^{(0)}\|f\|_{B^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\,,

for all
f\in B^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)
.

(5)

For any
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\big\|\big(\eta_{(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\big\|^{p}_{X^{\gamma}_{p}}\lesssim_{N}\|f\|_{X^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}^{p}\,,

(A.18)

where
N=N(\mathcal{I},\eta)>0
.
If
\eta
additionally satisfies

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Big[\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\eta(e^{n}t)\Big]>0\,,

(A.19)

then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{X^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}^{p}\lesssim_{N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\big\|\big(\eta_{(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\big\|^{p}_{X^{\gamma}_{p}}\,,

(A.20)

where
N=N(\mathcal{I},\eta)>0
.

(6)

For any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\rho}^{\,s}f\|_{X^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\simeq_{\mathcal{I},s}\|f\|_{X^{\gamma}_{p,\theta+sp}(\Omega)}\,.

(A.21)

(7)

For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|f\|_{X^{\gamma}_{p,\theta}(\Omega)}\simeq_{\mathcal{I},k}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\|D^{i}f\|_{X^{\gamma-k}_{p,\theta+ip}(\Omega)}.

(8)

For a fixed constant
A>1
, if
f
is distribution on
\Omega
and
f
is supported on
\{x\in\Omega\,:\,A^{-1}\leq\rho(x)\leq A\}
, then
f\in X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
if and only if
f\in X_{p}^{\gamma}
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\simeq_{\mathcal{I},A}\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}\,.

(9)

Let
t\in(0,1)
, and let
p_{i}\in(1,\infty),\,\theta_{i},\,\gamma_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
(
i=1,\,2,\,t
) are constants satisfying (
A.10
).
Then

\|f\|_{X_{p_{t},\theta_{t}}^{\gamma_{t}}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{N}\|f\|_{X_{p_{0},\theta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0}}(\Omega)}^{1-t}\|f\|_{X_{p_{1},\theta_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}(\Omega)}^{t}\,.

Proof of Proposition
A.3
.

(1) This follows from that
H_{p}^{\gamma}\subset B_{p}^{\gamma}
if
p\geq 2
, and
B_{p}^{\gamma}\subset H_{p}^{\gamma}
if
1<p\leq 2
(see,
e.g.
,
[
70
, Proposition 2.3.2/2.(iii)]
).

(2) This follows from that
X_{p}^{\gamma}\subset H_{p}^{s}\cap B_{p}^{s}
(see,
e.g.
,
[
70
, Proposition 2.3.2/2.(ii)]
).

(3) Note that
p_{0}<p_{1}
.
Since
X_{p_{0}}^{\gamma_{0}}\subset X_{p_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}\cap B_{p_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}
(see,
e.g.
,
[
70
, Theorem 2.7.1]
), we have

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta_{1}}\|\big(f\zeta_{0,(n)}\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{X_{p_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}}^{p_{1}}\Big)^{1/p_{1}}+\Big(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta_{1}}\|\big(f\zeta_{0,(n)}\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{B_{p_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}}^{p_{1}}\Big)^{1/p_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle N\Big(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta_{0}}\|\big(f\zeta_{0,(n)}\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{X_{p_{0}}^{\gamma_{0}}}^{p_{1}}\Big)^{1/p_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle N\Big(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta_{0}}\|\big(f\zeta_{0,(n)}\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{X_{p_{0}}^{\gamma_{0}}}^{p_{0}}\Big)^{1/p_{0}}\,,

where
N=N(d,p_{i},\gamma_{i};i=0,1)
.

(4)
If either
k\geq|\gamma|
and
X=H
or
k>|\gamma|
and
X=B
, then for any
f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\omega)
and
a\in C^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle\|af\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}\lesssim_{d,p,\gamma}\|a\|_{C^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|f\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}\,.

(A.22)

From direct calculation, one can observe that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
a\in C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{k}(\Omega)
,

\|a(e^{n}\cdot\,)\zeta_{1,(n)}\|_{C^{k}}\leq N(d,k)|a|_{k}^{(0)}\,.

By (
A.1
) and (
A.22
), we have

\displaystyle\|af\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}^{p}\,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|a(e^{n}\cdot\,)\zeta_{1,(n)}\cdot f(e^{n}\cdot\,)\zeta_{0,(n)}\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{I},k}

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}|a|_{k}^{(0)}\|f(e^{n}\cdot\,)\zeta_{0,(n)}\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}=|a|_{k}^{(0)}\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}^{p}\,.

(5) (
A.18
) is directly implied by (
A.6
).
To prove the second assertion, we assume (
A.19
).
Put
\eta_{0}(t):=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\eta(e^{n}t)
, so that

\displaystyle\eta_{0}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})\,\,,\,\,\,\,\eta_{0}(e\,\cdot\,)=\eta_{0}(\,\cdot\,)\,\,,\,\,\text{and}\quad\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big(\eta/\eta_{0}\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)=1\quad\text{on}\quad\mathbb{R}_{+}\,.

(A.23)

(
A.23
) implies that there exists
K\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sum_{|k|\leq K}\big(\eta/\eta_{0}\big)(e^{k}\cdot)=1\quad\text{on}\,\,\,[e^{-1},e]\,.

Therefore we obtain that

\displaystyle\zeta_{0,(n)}=\zeta_{0,(n)}\sum_{|k|\leq K}(\eta/\eta_{0})_{n-k}=\sum_{|k|\leq K}\eta_{(n-k)}\frac{\zeta_{0,(n)}}{\eta_{0,(n-k)}}=\sum_{|k|\leq K}\eta_{(n-k)}\big(\zeta_{0}/\eta_{0}\big)_{(n)}\,,

(A.24)

where the last inequality follows from the definition of
\eta_{0}
.
By (
A.1
), (
A.5
), and (
A.24
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\sum_{|k|\leq K}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\big(\zeta_{0}/\eta_{0}\big)_{(n)}(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\big(\eta_{(n-k)}f\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\sum_{|k|\leq K}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\big(\eta_{(n-k)}f\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\sum_{|k|\leq K}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\big(\eta_{(n-k)}f\big)(e^{n-k}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\big(\eta_{(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\,\cdot\,)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\gamma,\theta,K)
.
By (
A.17
) and (
A.23
), the proof is completed.

(6) Put
\eta(t)=t^{s}\zeta_{0}(t)
. Due to (
A.2
), we have

\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\eta(e^{n}t)>0\,.

Since

\widetilde{\rho}(x)^{s}\zeta_{0,(n)}(x)=e^{ns}\big(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(x)\big)^{s}\zeta_{0}(e^{-n}\widetilde{\rho}(x))=e^{ns}\eta_{(n)}(x)\,,

(
A.21
) is implied by (5) of this proposition.

(7) We only need to prove for
k=1
.
Note that

\displaystyle\|\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}\simeq_{d,p,\gamma}\|\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma-1}}+e^{n}\|\big(D(\zeta_{0,(n)}f)\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma-1}}\,.

(A.25)

By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}D\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}f\big)=\zeta_{0,(n)}\big(Df\big)+e^{-n}(\zeta_{0}^{\prime})_{(n)}(D\widetilde{\rho})f\,.\end{split}

(A.26)

By (
A.18
) and (4) of this proposition with (
A.4
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\big((\zeta_{0}^{\prime})_{(n)}(D\widetilde{\rho})f\big)(e^{n}\cdot))\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma-1}}^{p}\lesssim_{N}\|(D\widetilde{\rho})f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma-1}(\Omega)}\lesssim_{N}\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma-1}(\Omega)}\,,

(A.27)

where
N=N(d,p,\theta,\gamma)
.
By combining (
A.25
) - (
A.27
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}^{p}

\displaystyle\simeq_{\mathcal{I}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\Big(\|\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}f\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma-1}}^{p}+e^{n}\|\big(D(\zeta_{0,(n)}f)\big)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma-1}}^{p}\Big)

\displaystyle\simeq_{\mathcal{I}}\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma-1}(\Omega)}^{p}+\|Df\|_{X_{p,\theta+p}^{\gamma-1}(\Omega)}\,.

(8) Let
N_{0}
be the constant in (
A.4
), and take
B\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\sum_{|n|\leq B}\zeta_{0}(e^{n}\cdot)\equiv 1\quad\text{on}\quad[(2N_{0}A)^{-1},2N_{0}A]\,,

so that

\sum_{|n|\leq B}\zeta_{0,(n)}\equiv 1\quad\text{on}\,\,E:=\{x\in\Omega\,:\,(2A)^{-1}\leq\rho(x)\leq 2A\}\,.

Let
f
be a distribution on
\Omega
and supported on
\{x\in\Omega\,:\,A^{-1}\leq\rho(x)\leq A\}
.
Then
f
is also a distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Since
f\zeta_{0,(n)}\equiv 0
for all
|n|>B
, it follows from (
A.1
) that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)}^{p}=\sum_{|n|\leq B}e^{n\theta}\|(\zeta_{0,(n)}f)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}\leq N\,\|f\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\|f\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}=\|\sum_{|n|\leq B}\big(\zeta_{0,(n)}f\big)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}\leq N\sum_{|n|\leq B}e^{n\theta}\|(\zeta_{0,(n)}f)(e^{n}\cdot)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}\leq N\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}}^{p}\,,

where
N=N(d,p,\theta,\gamma,B)
.

(9) Due to Proposition
A.2
.(4) and the interpolation theory (see,
e.g.
,
[
69
, Theorem 1.9.3/(f)]
and its proof), we obtain that for any
f\in X_{p_{0},\theta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0}}(\Omega)\cap X_{p_{1},\theta_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{X_{p_{t}}^{\gamma_{t}}(\Omega)}\leq N\|f\|_{\left[X_{p_{0},\theta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0}}(\Omega),X_{p_{1},\theta_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}(\Omega)\right]_{t}}\leq\|f\|_{X_{p_{0}}^{\gamma_{0}}}^{1-t}\|f\|_{X_{p_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}}^{t}\,,

where
N=N(d,p_{i},\theta_{i},\gamma_{i},t;i=1,\,2)
and
\big[X_{p_{0},\theta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0}}(\Omega),X_{p_{1},\theta_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}(\Omega)\big]_{t}
is the complex interpolation space of
X_{p_{0},\theta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0}}(\Omega)
and
X_{p_{1},\theta_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}(\Omega)
.
Therefore the proof is completed.
∎

Remark A.4
.

As stated in
[
57
, Proposition 2.2.4]
, Proposition
A.3
.(5) can be generalized as the following:

•

Let
\{\eta_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subset C^{\infty}(\Omega)
satisfies that

(1)

There exists a constant
\alpha>1
,
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\mathrm{supp}(\eta_{n})\subset\{x\in\Omega\,:\,\alpha^{n-k_{0}}<\rho(x)<\alpha^{n+k_{0}}\}\qquad\forall\quad n\in\mathbb{Z}\,;

(2)

There exist
\{N_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\sup\limits_{\Omega}|D^{m}\eta_{n}|\leq N_{m}\alpha^{nm}
.

Then for any
u\in X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
, (
A.18
) holds for
\{\eta_{n}\}
instead of
\{\eta_{(n)}\}
(where
N
in (
A.18
) depends only on
d
,
p
,
\theta
,
\gamma
,
\alpha
,
k_{0}
,
\{N_{m}\}
).
Moreover, if there exists
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\eta_{n}\geq\epsilon_{0}
on
\Omega
, then
(
A.20
) holds for
\{\eta_{n}\}
(resp.
\alpha^{n},\alpha^{n\theta}
) instead of
\{\eta_{(n)}\}
(resp.
e^{n}
,
e^{n\theta}
), (where
N
in (
A.20
) depends only on
d
,
p
,
\theta
,
\gamma
,
\alpha
,
k_{0}
,
\{N_{m}\}
,
\epsilon_{0}
).

The proof of this statement is almost same with the proof of Proposition
A.3
.(5); note that there exists
K\in\mathbb{N}
depending only on
\alpha
and
k_{0}
such that for any
n_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle\#\{n\in\mathbb{Z}\,:\,\left[e^{n-1},e^{n+1}\right]\cap\left[\alpha^{n_{0}-k_{0}},\alpha^{n_{0}+k_{0}}\right]\neq\emptyset\}

\displaystyle\leq K\,;

\displaystyle\#\{n\in\mathbb{Z}\,:\,\left[\alpha^{n-k_{0}},\alpha^{n+k_{0}}\right]\cap\left[e^{n_{0}-1},e^{n_{0}+1}\right]\neq\emptyset\}

\displaystyle\leq K\,,

where
\#A
is the number of elements in a set
A
.
The above statement implies that if
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
satisfies (
A.19
), then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d})}^{p}\simeq\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{n\theta}\|\eta(x_{1})f(e^{n}x)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}\qquad\forall\quad f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+})\,,

(A.28)

and if
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
satisfies (
A.19
) for
2^{n}
instead of
e^{n}
, then

\displaystyle\|g\|_{X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\})}^{p}\simeq\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{n\theta}\|\eta(|x|)g(2^{n}x)\|_{X_{p}^{\gamma}}^{p}\qquad\forall\quad g\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\})\,.

(A.29)

In
[
45
,
46
,
48
]
, the space
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+})
is defined by (
A.28
).
In addition, in
[
53
]
the space
H_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}\big(\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\big)
is defined by (
A.29
).

A.2.
Auxiliary results

Lemma A.5
.

Let
p\in(1,\infty)
,
\gamma,\,\theta\in\mathbb{R}
.
There exist linear maps

\Lambda_{i}\,:\,X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega)\quad,\quad i=0,\,1,\,\ldots,\,d\,,

such that for any
f\in X_{p,\theta}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
,

f=\Lambda_{0}f+\sum_{i=1}^{d}D_{i}(\Lambda_{i}f)

and
